This text belongs to a genre of Sanskrit literature known as "Yuddha-Jayotsava" or military astrology, which provides kings with omens, planetary alignments, and rituals to ensure success in conflict.</note>

<meta>This page transitions from the archival cover page seen previously to the actual commencement of the manuscript. It begins with the traditional invocation and the mythological framework—a dialogue between the god Shiva and the goddess Parvati—that introduces the "Science of Breath" (Swarodaya) and various astrological charts.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
<header>4. The rising of breath is the supreme secret, rare even for the sages. O Lord, it was previously told by me for the benefit of mortals of limited intellect. 5. <note>original: "Swarodayam param guhyam..." This verse numbers 4 and 5 suggest we are at the beginning of a specific chapter or section within the larger work.</note></header>

<margin>
Swa.
8.
1
<note>Likely an abbreviation for "Swarodaya," followed by the page or section number.</note>
</margin>

**Om. Prosperity! Salutations to the illustrious Ganesha. Salutations to the illustrious Purushottama.** <note>Ganesha is the remover of obstacles; Purushottama refers to the Supreme Being, often Vishnu.</note> Om. Salutations to that Sun of Omniscience, who, with the rays of knowledge, rescues men submerged in the darkness of delusion. 

The God of Gods, the Teacher of the World, was seated upon the peak of Mount Kailasha, surrounded by hosts of divine attendants <term>Ganas</term> and served by assemblies of sages. There, the Goddess Parvati, the beloved of her husband, bowed her head to her spouse and questioned the Supreme Lord. 

**The Goddess said:** O Blessed One, God of Gods, whose crown is adorned by the moon, tell me the supreme knowledge to dispel ignorance, O Great Lord. Explain the science of poets, the vowels and letters, and the rising of the breath <term>Swarodaya</term>. Tell me of the zodiac signs and planets, their forms, and their relative strengths and weaknesses. Tell me of the different divisions of the zodiac, and through them, the good and evil, profit and loss, happiness and misery, and even life and death. 

O Lord, speak of victory and defeat, of coming and going, of thoughts held in the mind or objects held in the fist. Explain the auspicious and inauspicious signs determined through the practice of bodily breath <term>Shvasayoga</term>, and by the division of the letters of a name. O Best of Gods, tell me of the classification of names by their lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> and zodiac signs. 

Explain, O Shankara, the science of life and the Wheel of Time <term>Kalachakra</term> through the various grid diagrams <term>Shalaka-chakra</term>, and according to the instructions of the Guru. Provide a definitive and doubt-free explanation of birth charts, the Five Wheels <term>Panchachakra</term>, the Eight-fold Wheels, and the Wheel of Longevity. O Great Lord, explain the Wheel of Death governed by the planets, the castes and lunar mansions, the remaining twenty-five stars, and the Throne of the Stars. 

Tell me of the Wheel of Distance, the Pot-shaped Wheel <term>Kalasha-chakra</term>, the Star Wheel, the Fort Wheel <term>Kota-chakra</term>, and the Eagle Wheel <term>Garuda-chakra</term>. O Lord, speak of the many wonders of the Name-Letter-Number system, <insert>tell me</insert> <unclear>the letters of the name</unclear> and the results of examining the hours <term>Hora</term> and the positions of the stars. O Lord of Gods, O Shankara, if you are pleased with me, reveal the rising of the names.

Thus addressed by Parvati, the God of Gods, the Great Lord Maheshvara, spoke these words with pleasure: "Listen, Parvati, to the final decision." **The Blessed Lord [said:]**

-> The Wheel of Auspicious Victory, O Lord: 2 <-
<note>original: "Sambhadrajayachakramaprabho". This likely refers to the second specific diagram or chapter regarding military victory.</note>

<summary>This page marks the formal opening of the text, framed as a dialogue where the Goddess Parvati asks Lord Shiva to reveal the secrets of Swarodaya (breath science) and various astrological wheels (chakras) used to predict everything from life expectancy to victory in battle.</summary>

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Lord Shiva and the Goddess Parvati. Following Parvati's request for divine wisdom, Shiva begins to reveal the secrets of the Swarodaya (Science of Breath) and its application in predicting success, failure, and the movement of time.</meta>

<page-num>653</page-num>

**He spoke:**

In proper order, I shall clearly relate to you the characteristics of all the charts <term>chakras</term> and the qualities of those who are absorbed in them, O beloved of the world. This knowledge brings immediate conviction <note>original: "sadyah pratyayakarakam"; refers to knowledge that can be verified by personal experience immediately.</note> and was spoken at the dawn of the **Kali Age**. During the hair-raising battle between the gods and the demons, this science was created by the gods themselves for the sake of victory and defeat, O praised by heroes. 

This scripture is a great secret, revealed to you for your benefit. I have not revealed this to others without distinction, O **Parvati**. It resides within the self and provides immediate proof; it is more secret than the most secret thing, O Goddess, and must be guarded with great effort. 

Whosoever among men desires to know the rising of the breath <note>original: "Swarodayam"; the practice of observing the flow of breath through the nostrils to determine auspicious timing.</note>... he becomes like the infinite sky, O **Parvati**. At what time does the Blessed One shine? Through the vision of the breath, one may disregard all auspicious and inauspicious omens <term>nimittas</term> on one side, and focus solely on the mind’s stillness. 

One should abandon the practice of chanting <term>japa</term> if it is not superior to the omens found within the subtle body <note>The "subtle body" refers to the energetic anatomy (nadis and chakras) rather than the physical flesh.</note>. The body is composed of the nectar of radiance and provides the fruits of the three times <note>Past, present, and future.</note>. Therefore, one should always contemplate whether an event will be auspicious or inauspicious by using this breath. 

In the gathering of armies, in the excitement of the battlefield, or amidst the loud shouts of disputants, one who is successful in this practice remains prosperous. Conversely, when omens are reversed, one should seek atonement for oneself. 

The movements of the planets and the various stations of the stars are all seen within this science. The favorable rising of Aries and the other signs is seen always in the five pathways of the breath. He who is an expert in the practice of yoga <term>yogābhyāsa</term>... such people who are endowed with this knowledge and focused on the correct time are rarely seen. Therefore, I have shown this here in great detail... the chariot of fire... the piercing of the middle path... thus says the scripture. <gloss>it is so</gloss>

<summary>Lord Shiva explains to Parvati that the Science of Breath (Swarodaya) is a supreme secret that grants immediate proof of its efficacy, allowing a practitioner to predict victory, understand the three divisions of time, and transcend external omens through internal mastery.</summary>
<keywords>Swarodaya, Science of Breath, Parvati, Shiva, Kali Age, omens, subtle body, yoga practice, victory, astrology</keywords>

He moves to the left, O Goddess. When situated in their own positions, they reside in the left and right sides. In the two divisions <term>tattika</term>, there are thirty-six...

...prepared for enjoyment. In the slow time, it becomes half; half of that again at the end; half of that again. For the **Sun**, or the son of the **Sun** <note>Saturn</note>, or for the **Moon**, or for **Venus**...
...half of that, and half of that again for **Mercury**. Similarly, in their own divisions, and in the divisions of the **Moon** after that. Thus, through the marrow, one should understand the time-measures <term>chashakas</term> <note>original: "chashaka"; literally 'a cup' or 'goblet', here used as a specific unit of time in breath-counting.</note>. 
...the mind. Half of a time-measure is twenty-seven. For our enemies and poets, half of that, and half again—thus twenty-seven... in this way, indeed...
<margin>This much</margin> ...when the time-measure is declared to be in **Pisces** <note>original: "meena"</note>. The horizontal movement is like the wind. When **Rahu** and **Jupiter** are present, then, O Goddess, the work of men must be carried out...
<margin>This much</margin> <gloss>Rahu</gloss> ...O Beloved, he who remains in the middle of the months provides happiness. Whatever occurs in this union is said to be the destroyer of kingdoms. As it has been said:
"The work must be performed with the five-fold waters." When **Rahu** arrives at an auspicious time, the union of actions is auspicious and purified. In the *Svaratna*, life is said to be...
...very beautiful. Among the groups of equinoctial movements, O Goddess, it is declared to be middling. Knowing this, the wise person should perform actions as desired. This...

**The Resplendent Goddess said:**
O Blessed One, God of Gods, illuminator of all knowledge, I wish to hear the resolution to my doubt...

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Lord (Shiva) and the Goddess (Parvati) regarding the Science of Breath and Sound. While the previous page focused on planetary locations within the body, this page transitions into the "Svaranirṇaya" or the Determination of Vowels, linking phonetics to the five elements and stages of human life.</meta>

<page-num>४</page-num>

<margin>Vowels 1</margin> ...in the *Swarodaya* <note>The "Rise of Breath/Sound," a divinatory science centered on the flow of breath through the nostrils and its relationship to cosmic rhythms.</note>, as it is told: How many letters are declared? How many letters are divided by long and short forms? How is the mixture of white and black <note>original: "shuklakrishna"; referring to the waxing and waning lunar fortnights or the polarities of the breath</note> known in the throats of humans by the astrologer <note>original: "daivajña"</note>? The determination of vowels—tell me all of this in brief.

**The Lord Spoke:**
Listen, O Goddess, I shall explain as you have requested of me: the determination of vowels, O Auspicious One, and the origin of letters. **A** <note>original: "akāra"</note>, **I** <note>original: "ikāra"</note>, and **U** <note>original: "ukāra"</note> as the third; **E** <note>original: "ekāra"</note>, O Goddess of the Gods, and **O** <note>original: "okāra"</note> as the fifth. These five vowels <term>swaras</term> are proclaimed, by which this entire universe is pervaded.

The elements <term>panchabhuta</term>, starting with Earth, are proclaimed for the embodied soul. Smell, taste, form, sound, and touch are remembered in their respective order. These five vowels, O Great Goddess, are the revealers of all actions. The five long vowels <term>dirgha</term> are spoken of in the exact same manner.

The fifty letters from *Ka* to *Ha* are proclaimed in the various groups. In the division of white and black, they proceed upward, O Beautiful One. According to the number of letters, the vowel should be known in the groups.

<margin>Short</margin> O Sage, the long and short vowels are determined through their internal relationship with those [letters], like the full white [moon] in order. *Jaya*, *Nanda*, and others... <margin>To be avoided: 4</margin> ...the symbols of vowels, even in the short scripts... Ri, Li, Lli <note>These are specialized vocalic sounds in Sanskrit phonetics.</note> ...determined by their placement. Each name possesses a treasury. ...There is no doubt that similar results follow.

**Child** <term>bala</term>, **Maiden** <term>kumari</term>, **Young Woman** <term>yuvati</term>, and **Old Woman** <term>sthavira</term> <note>These four categories represent the "age" or potency of a sound, used to predict the strength of an outcome.</note> are told in order. Five scripts are mentioned for these. In that context... in the destruction of the treasury... comes the arrival of wealth according to one's own vowel. This should be known, O Goddess, regarding the vowels...

->Hla: Hla: Hla: [Calculation or seed syllables] with two, four...<-

<summary>This page defines the five primary vowels (A, I, U, E, O) as the foundational vibrations of the universe, linking them to the five elements and the sensory world. It also introduces the classification of sounds by "age" (child to old woman) to determine their prophetic potency.</summary>
<keywords>Swarodaya, Vowels, Five Elements, Phonetics, Divination, Shiva, Parvati, Tattva</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the "Svarodaya" (Science of Breath and Sound). While the previous page established the existence of five primary vowels (A, I, U, E, O) and their elemental correspondences, this page provides a complex calendrical system. It maps these vowels to specific lunar days (tithis), planetary rulers, and cardinal directions to determine favorable or unfavorable omens.</meta>

<page-num>३</page-num>

<warning>A white paper patch is pasted over the top center of the page, potentially obscuring parts of the first line. There are some minor ink smudges and faded text on the right edge.</warning>

<margin>
Self <note>original: "Sva"</note>
Dam <note>likely an abbreviation for a specific tantric or astrological category</note>
3
</margin>

...there should be <unclear>rise</unclear>. Indeed, each one is declared for the seven days <note>original: "saptadinā"</note>. This which I have told you, O Auspicious One, is the gross form; now listen again to the sequence of its expansion. O Goddess, I shall describe that sequence:

In the waxing fortnight <note>original: "shukla-paksha"</note>, the rise of the vowel **A** <note>original: "akāra"</note> occurs on the **Nanda** <note>The 'Joyous' lunar days</note> dates, which are the first, sixth, and eleventh days.

On the second, seventh, and twelfth days—in these dates known as **Bhadra** <note>The 'Auspicious' lunar days</note>—one should recognize the rise of the vowel **I** <note>original: "ikāra"</note> in the science of breath.

On the third, eighth, and thirteenth days—in these dates known as **Jaya** <note>The 'Victorious' lunar days</note>—one should recognize the rise of the vowel **U** <note>original: "ukāra"</note>, O Dear One.

On the fourth, ninth, and likewise the fourteenth days—in these dates known as **Rikta** <note>The 'Empty' lunar days; traditionally considered inauspicious for new beginnings</note>—thus occurs the rise of the vowel **E** <note>original: "ekāra"</note>.

On the fifth, <insert>tenth</insert>, and fifteenth days—know these as the **Purna** <note>The 'Full' or 'Complete' lunar days</note> dates—the rise of the vowel **O** <note>original: "okāra"</note> occurs.

O Goddess, in the waxing fortnight, it is said that the names of the dates correspond to the vowels starting with 'A' and so on. The lunar dates categorized as Nanda, Bhadra, Jaya, Rikta, and Purna proceed in order. Their planetary rulers are Mars, Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn <note>original: "Ruja-Soma-Jña-Vāgīsha-Sita-Sūryasuta"</note> in respective order.

Whether an outcome is auspicious or inauspicious is determined by these. The directions—East, South, West, North, and the Center—correspond to them in order.

If Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> is in the house of a **Nanda** date, but the deity of the constellation <term>Nakshatra</term> is not present there, then the vowel **A** loses its power of rise.

If the Moon <note>original: "Soma"</note> is in the house of a **Bhadra** date, but the Serpent Deity <note>original: "Sarpa-daivata"; referring to the Ashlesha constellation</note> is not present, then the vowel **I** is affected.

If Mercury <note>original: "Jña"</note> is in the house of a **Jaya** date, and its deity is likewise present, then the rise of the vowel **U** occurs.

If Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is in the house of a **Rikta** date, and the deity is present there, then the rise of the vowel **E** occurs.

If Saturn <note>original: "Sauri"</note> is in the house of a **Purna** date, and the deity is present there, then the rise of the vowel **O** occurs.

The destruction of the vowel **A**...

3
</margin>

<summary>This page details the specific correlations between the five primary vowels and the lunar calendar (tithis), categorizing days into five types (Nanda, Bhadra, etc.) and assigning them planetary rulers and directions. It explains how the "rise" or potency of a vowel is contingent upon the alignment of planets and presiding deities.</summary>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page concludes the first chapter of the "Upachaya Chakra" (The Wheel of Increase), a text on Svarodaya (the science of breath and sound). It transitions from the technical rules regarding vowel sounds and their effects on success or failure into a dialogue between the Goddess Parvati and Lord Shiva regarding the structure of the zodiac and its relationship to the human body.</meta>

-> **Sya** <-

In the event of the rise of the vowel **A** <note>original: "akāra"</note>, the destruction of **A** occurs. From the rise of the vowel **U**, the destruction of **I** occurs. In the same way, when the vowel **E** rises, it is for the sake of wealth. When **O** has risen, the same applies to the triad of **O**. When **M** has completed its rise, this is the timing for the rise of the group <margin>tna</margin>. This has been shown through a general method. Having heard of the specific distinctions even within those lunar dates <note>original: "tithis"</note>, I shall tell you of them, O Parvati. Everywhere, this knowledge shines specifically through these beneficial methods, especially in this great teaching on Deceiving Time <note>original: "Kalavanchana"; a tantric practice to prolong life or foresee death</note>.

**Thus ends the first chapter, titled "The Science of Breath," in the "Wheel of Increase" composed by Shri Punjaraja. 1**

**The Holy Goddess said:**
O Lord of the Gods, you have explained the science of breath <note>original: "Svarodaya"</note> to me in detail. Now I wish to hear about the repository in the lunar orb <note>original: "shashimandale"</note>, and whatever is well-known regarding gains and losses, victory and defeat. O Lord of the Gods, you whose banner is the bull <note>original: "Vrishabhadhvaja"; an epithet for Shiva</note>, tell me in detail the characteristics of the planets, the zodiac signs, and the constellations. O Great Lord, speak of the divisions of exaltation and debilitation.

**The Lord said:**
Listen, my dear, to the complete essence of the zodiac signs. I shall tell you everything about the planets and constellations. It is said there are twelve signs resting upon the zodiac circle. There are also seven planets, O Gauri; hear their names from me. The wheel of the stars <note>original: "Bhachakra"</note> is celebrated as the very body of Time <note>original: "Kala"</note>. In that body, divided among the strikes <note>likely referring to astrological divisions</note> and objects, are the head, the face, the arms, the heart, the stomach, the waist, the bladder, the genitals, the thighs, the knees, and the feet.

The signs are: **Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius,** and **Pisces**. <note>original Sanskrit names: Mesha, Vrisha, Mithuna, Karka, Simha, Kumarika, Tula, Kita, Dhanvi, Mriga, Kumbha, Jhasha</note>.

**Venus, Mercury, the Moon, the Sun, Mercury <note>represented by two different names: Budha and Jna</note>, Venus <note>as Bhargava</note>, Mars, Jupiter,** and **Saturn** are the lords of the signs.

Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer <unclear>mi</unclear>...

<summary>This page concludes Chapter 1 of Punjaraja’s work on the science of breath and initiates a dialogue where Shiva begins teaching Parvati about the Zodiac signs (Rashis) and their correspondence to the "Body of Time" (Kalapurusha).</summary>

When one is troubled or weary, starting from the East and concluding with the North-East <note>original: "ishana"</note>, one should combine the fifth and ninth positions. # Cruel signs are called male, while the auspicious ones are known as female, O Beloved. O my Dear, listen now to the attainment of the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note> through these divisions. O Great Goddess, listen to the six-fold system <note>original: "shatpadayi"</note> of the zodiac signs.

# O Dear, the signs beginning with Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> are known respectively as male and female, and as Moveable, Fixed, and Dual-natured. They grant results according to their names. O Great Goddess, O Gauri, listen to the truth of the six-fold division of matter. These six are to be known as: **the Hora, the Drekkana, the Navamsha, the Dwadashamsha, and the Trishamsha.**

These three are spoken of first, then the <term>Hora</term> <note>a division of a sign into two halves (15° each)</note> and the <term>Drekkana</term> <note>a division of a sign into three parts (10° each)</note> are described. Its third part is the Drekkana, and the ninth part is the <term>Navamsha</term> <note>the most important sub-chart, dividing a sign into nine parts of 3°20'</note>. O Great Goddess, the twelfth part is known as the <term>Dwadashamsha</term> <note>a division of 2°30'</note>. The <term>Trishamsha</term> <note>a division of a sign into 30 degrees, though often used to refer to specific degree-stints assigned to planets</note> is perfected as the last division. One should calculate the Hora and Drekkana according to their own measures and forms, as stated here. One should attain those divisions through the portions of the signs, their respective positions, and the lords of the houses. The results of the lords of the Hora and other divisions are as follows: In the "odd" signs, O Goddess, the first Hora belongs to the Sun <note>original: "Indurochishah," literally 'light-bringer'</note> and the second to the Moon. In the "even" signs, it is said to be the reverse. The lords of the first, fifth, and ninth signs are always said to be the lords of the Drekkanas for all signs, O Parvati. Now listen to the calculation of the Navamshas for Aries and the others, divided properly: **For Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius, the Navamshas begin with Aries. For Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn, they are known to begin with Capricorn. For Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius, the Navamshas begin with Libra. For Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces, the Navamshas begin with Cancer.** O Goddess, the <term>Vargottama</term> <note>literally "best division"; refers to a planet occupying the same sign in both the main chart and the Navamsha sub-chart</note> is known as such when the Navamsha matches the sign. This is a giver of auspicious results. For all signs, the Dwadashamshas are calculated starting from the sign itself. In Aries and Taurus... <note>text becomes fragmented regarding specific degree assignments</note> five, five, eight...

<header><unclear>In the seven divisions, among the zodiac signs, the 30th parts (Trishamsha) of the Vasus and others in even signs</unclear></header>

# The Nature of the Signs and Their Divisions

The thirty parts <note>original: "trishamsha"</note> of the signs are declared in the seven houses; for the even signs, these are assigned to the Vasus <note>a group of eight elemental deities, here representing specific planetary rulers of these divisions</note> and others. Thus, the six categories of matter <note>the Shadvarga system mentioned on the previous page</note> have been declared in brief.

O Beloved, among the signs, some are known as "Night-signs." Some are "Day-signs." O Dear, the remaining signs are **Back-rising** <note>original: "Prishthodaya"; signs that rise with their rear first, usually considered less auspicious in certain contexts</note>. Those starting from the head are **Head-rising** <note>original: "Shirshodaya"</note>, while Pisces <note>original: "Meena"</note> is known as **Dual-rising** <note>original: "Ubhaya-sanjnaka"; rising with both ends</note>.

Virgo, Gemini, and Cancer are to be known as strong at specific times. Capricorn, Cancer, and Pisces are those that dwell in the water. Scorpio <note>original: "Keeta"</note> is strong at night and is designated as a place of refuge. 

### On the Strength of the Signs and Planets

A zodiac sign <note>original: "Rashi"</note> that is joined with its own Lord, or with Mercury <note>original: "Jnya"</note> or Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, or is aspected by them, is known to be **strong**; any other is considered devoid of strength. 

Aries, Taurus, Capricorn, Virgo, Cancer, and Pisces: these are the signs where the Sun and other planets attain their positions. O beautiful-faced one, listen to the degrees of **Highest Exaltation** <note>original: "Paramocchaka"</note>:
*   **Sun:** 10 degrees (in Aries)
*   **Moon:** 3 degrees (in Taurus)
*   **Mars:** 28 degrees (in Capricorn)
*   **Mercury:** 15 degrees (in Virgo)
*   **Jupiter:** 5 degrees (in Cancer)
*   **Venus:** 27 degrees (in Pisces)
*   **Saturn:** 20 degrees (in Libra)

<margin>**The auspicious planets are in the seventh; Scorpio and others are remembered as strong at night.**</margin>

The seventh sign from the exaltation point is the **Debilitation** <note>original: "Nicha"</note> sign. A planet in its exaltation has full strength, while in its debilitation, it is weak. 

<margin>**During the Northern Transit (Uttarayana), they are strong in the seventh position.**</margin>
<margin>Sun 4</margin>
<margin>**Those in the Southern Transit (Dakshinayana) and those that are Retrograde are known to be endowed with strength.**</margin>

### Directional Strength (Dig-bala)

In the seventh house, the Son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> is strong. In the tenth house, the Sun and the Son of the Earth <note>Mars</note> are strong. In the day, the Sun, Venus, and Jupiter are strong; the others are strong at night. Benefic planets are powerful during the **Bright Fortnight** <note>original: "Shukla-paksha"; the waxing moon</note>, while others are endowed with strength during the **Dark Fortnight** <note>original: "Krishna-paksha"; the waning moon</note>.

When the Sun moves in the **Northern Transit** <note>original: "Uttarayana"; the half of the year when the sun moves north</note>, the planets are...

->The strength of the day, the sign, the month, and the year increases accordingly. 1<-

Beginning with Venus, then the Moon, then the Sun: the planets are increasingly powerful in this order <note>This refers to *Naisargika Bala* or natural strength, where the Sun is naturally the most potent celestial body</note>. If their strengths appear equal, their natural dominance decides the outcome. The Sun, Jupiter, the Moon, and Mercury are [considered in one group]; then Venus, Mars, and the Sun. Mars, Jupiter, and the Sun [again], then Mars and the Sun are powerful. These are the friends <note>original: "mitrani"</note> in the various groups; those remaining are considered enemies.

In this way, the fate of men should be known, O highly successful one <note>Addressing Parvati</note>. Indeed, the lords of the earth are Jupiter and Mercury; the lords of the classes <note>original: "vargana"</note> are the fierce Mars and Venus, while Saturn is declared as the one who offers sacrifice.

The waning Moon, the Sun, Mars, and Saturn are **malefic** <note>original: "papa"; literally 'sinful' or 'cruel' in an astrological sense</note>. The others are **benefic** <note>original: "saumya"; gentle or auspicious</note>. The planet Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> becomes malefic when joined with malefics, and benefic when joined with benefics <note>Mercury is considered 'neutral' and takes on the quality of the planet it is currently associated with</note>.

Mars, Saturn, and Jupiter are male; the Moon and Venus <note>original: "bhargava"</note> are female; Mercury and Saturn are known as eunuchs by those who know the truth. Venus and Jupiter are Brahmins <note>the priestly/scholarly class</note>; the Sun and Mars are Kshatriyas <note>the warrior/ruling class</note>; the Moon and Mercury are Vaishyas <note>the merchant class</note>; and Saturn is Shudra <note>the laboring class</note>. Saturn is also considered of mixed caste.

The Sun and Moon are the Kings; Mars is the Commander-in-Chief, O Beloved; Jupiter and Venus are the two Ministers; Mercury is the Prince <note>original: "raja-putra"</note>; and the son of the Sun (Saturn) is the Servant. This is how the divisions of the planets are taught. Thus, the Sun and Jupiter are of the quality of Goodness <note>original: "sattva"</note>; Venus and the Moon are of Passion <note>original: "rajas"</note>; while the son of the Sun (Saturn) and Mars are of Ignorance/Inertia <note>original: "tamas"</note>. At the time of birth, a planet manifests its own inherent qualities.

### The Twelve Houses of the Horoscope

Beginning from the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note>, the twelve Houses <note>original: "Bhavas"</note> are to be known in order:
1. The Body <note>Physical appearance and self</note>
2. Wealth
3. Brothers <note>and courage</note>
4. Relatives and Friends
5. Children <note>and intelligence</note>
6. Enemies
7. Spouse <note>original: "jaya"</note>
8. Death <note>and longevity</note>
9. Dharma <note>religion and fortune</note>
10. Action <note>original: "karma"; career and public life</note>
11. Gains
12. Losses/Expenditure

The third, sixth, tenth, and eleventh houses are known as the **Upachaya** <note>Houses of "improvement" or "growth" where planets tend to get better over time</note>. Others are different. The ninth and fifth houses are declared as the **Trikona** <note>Trines; the most auspicious houses in a chart</note>. The fourth house is called the Abode <note>original: "hibuka/veshma"</note>. The four houses [1, 4, 7, 10] are the **Kendras** <note>Angular houses, the pillars of the horoscope</note>. The ninth is the Trine, and the third is...

<margin>
3rd row/line
</margin>

Jupiter, Moon, Mars, and Sun; Venus and Saturn. Saturn, Venus, and Mercury are established as friends and so forth. The Sun and Moon; the Moon and Saturn; Mars, Venus, and Saturn; likewise Saturn, Mars, and Jupiter; Jupiter and Saturn are similar. Venus, Mars, and Jupiter; Saturn and Jupiter are considered neutral <note>original: "sama"</note>. Thus are the lords of men, and the planets: enemies, friends, and neutrals. These should be known through the sign, day, month, and year for the desired gain.

# Now, the detailed movements through the twelve houses of death, the names of the Ascendants, and the verses on their results, O Parvati. 3

---

In the fifth house, one finds knowledge; in the seventh house, a friend <note>original: "mitra"; the 7th house usually represents partners or allies</note>. In those qualities, one is known as a scholar of the scriptures. That is how it is declared. In the eighth house, O Goddess, there is poverty.
<margin>Malefics in the houses of increase <note>term: "Upachaya"; these are the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses where malefic planets are said to provide growth and strength</note> are best; otherwise, they are condemned. 4 The opposite [is true for benefics].</margin> 

The twelfth is the same; likewise, the results for the fourth and eighth houses are declared. Regarding the second, fifth, tenth, and eleventh houses, the scholars of this science have explained the results there as being of a different <margin>strength and fruit</margin> nature. They know the third, sixth, ninth, and seventh as the "leaning" houses <note>term: "Apoklima"; these correspond to the 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses in Western astrology, representing a decline in power</note>. At this time, the pillars <note>term: "Kendra"; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which act as the main supports of a birth chart</note> are auspicious, and the trine houses <note>term: "Trikona"; the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, considered the most fortunate</note> contain the benefic planets. <margin>In house 4</margin> 

In the tenth and the third, according to what has been illustrated, and in the ninth, fifth, and sixth—O beautiful-faced one!—in the fourth and eighth, O Goddess, diseases caused by malefic influences <note>original: "papa rogah"</note> are declared. <margin>All 3</margin> 

In the seventh house, one should calculate the full results. O Ruler of the Gods, the highest planets do not look upon <note>term: "Drishti"; the "aspect" or gaze of a planet that projects its influence onto other houses</note> the remaining houses. Saturn <note>original: "Arki"; literally "son of the Sun"</note> looks upon the third and tenth houses <margin>1</margin> and bestows results. Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> looks upon the trines <note>the 5th and 9th houses</note>, while Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> looks upon the fourth and eighth. All the remaining planets look upon the seventh house from their position. In this very way, O illustrious one, 

the method of the zodiac circle <note>original: "Rashi-mandala"</note> has been shown in brief. This has been declared even for the malefic influences, my dear. Those whose minds are confused and whose senses are unbridled, who seek only their own interests through their daily work, fall into envy. But those who are devoted to the service of their Teacher <note>original: "Guru-bhakti"</note>, they understand the supreme secret; the rest are merely interested in the literal meaning of the scriptures.

Thus ends the second chapter titled **The Ripening of the Form** <note>original: "Vigraha Vipaka"; refers to how physical destiny or bodily karma manifests</note> within the sacred *Bhuja Patala* manual. 2

The Goddess said: O Holy One, Lord of the Gods, whose crest is adorned by the moon, tell me, O Supreme Lord, <insert>the knowledge of good and bad actions</insert>. O Lord, as I have acted previously, tell me out of your favor, if you are the compassionate one.

The Lord said: Listen, O Goddess, to the supreme knowledge, rare even for the sages. It is the best of all sciences and the giver of liberation. Having a joyful heart after knowing the science of expiations <note>original: "Prayashchitta"</note> mentioned before, and having performed the salutations to the Divine, one should offer the results to the Knower of Time.

**When the vessel/querent asks these questions**

**From the Ascendant of the divine, the outcome is seen.**

<margin>Consider this well.</margin>

At the time of the Ascendant <note>term: "Tatkalalagna"; the astrological sign rising on the eastern horizon at the exact moment a question is asked</note>, one should consider the two-fold results associated with the signs and the planetary strengths at that moment. Without this, one should not deliberate on the query. Benefic planets <note>term: "Saumyas"; literally "gentle" planets like Jupiter, Venus, and a well-associated Mercury</note> located in the Angular <note>term: "Kendra"; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> or Trine houses, and malefic planets <note>term: "Papas"; literally "sinful" or "cruel" planets like Saturn and Mars</note> located in the Houses of Increase <note>term: "Upachaya"; the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</note>, grant great success. If they are placed in the opposite manner, the results are condemned and bring failure.

When the Sun or even equal planets are situated in the tenth, eleventh, or second house from the Ascendant, the seeker obtains wealth and the fulfillment of their signs. There is no doubt about this. If the Ascendant is in a Moveable sign <note>term: "Chara Rashi"; signs like Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn which indicate change and motion</note> and is occupied by a malefic planet, it indicates the destruction of the object of the quest, O beautiful-faced one <note>note: a traditional address to the Goddess Parvati, the listener of this wisdom</note>. However, if the Lord of the Ascendant aspects <note>term: "Drishti"; the astrological "gaze" or influence one planet or house has over another</note> the Ascendant, or if it is aspected by a friend or a benefic in the fourth house, then one should predict the attainment of wealth and success for the querent.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological houses, specifically applying the principles of house placement to military expeditions and the outcomes for a ruler or commander. It builds on the previous page's definitions of the twelve houses by providing rules for predicting victory, defeat, and the movement of enemy forces.</meta>

<margin>
Own 8 <note>likely referring to the eighth house or a numerical strength value</note>
</margin>

If malefic planets are situated in the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>original: Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the query</note> or in the <term>House of Death</term> <note>original: Nidhana; the eighth house</note>, or if they are placed in the fourth house, then O Goddess, the arrival of the King shall occur. If <term>benefic planets</term> <note>original: Saumya-graha; "gentle" planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a waxing Moon</note> are located in the eighth or the second house, the person attains a long life, as they do when Venus is so positioned. If <term>malefic planets</term> <note>original: Papa; "cruel" planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are in the Ascendant or the eighth house while in their signs of debilitation or in the signs of their enemies, one should predict death or great sin for the seeker. 

Malefics positioned in the Ascendant bring pain; in the fourth, they cause conflict; in the seventh, they lead to the destruction of wealth; and in the tenth, they cause the failure of one's actions. In this way, O Goddess, the "Entry" <note>likely referring to the arrival of an army or a new phase of a campaign</note> is described. If the Ascendant or the eighth house contains malefics, but the seventh house contains benefics, or if planets are in the third, sixth, tenth, or eleventh houses <note>the houses of increase or Upachaya</note>, they provide protection and grant auspiciousness. 

Conversely, if malefics are situated in the "cruel corners" <note>likely the intermediate cardinal directions or specific unlucky house divisions</note>, the King will become afflicted by disease. The seventh house is the place of the spouse, and the second house is where "cruel city-dwellers" are said to be seen <note>an interpretation of urban disturbances or civil unrest</note>. In the ninth house, O Goddess, diseases are noted. Those who possess auspicious portions <note>Vargas/divisions</note> in their charts should be granted the prediction of victory. 

Defeat is predicted when the planets are associated with malefics or are weak in their movements. Those who are wise and energetic among the invaders will find success through these yogas <note>astrological combinations</note>, while their enemies will be conquered. If benefics are in the Ascendant, the seventh, and the eighth houses, the enemy army is certainly defeated at the time of crisis. 

If Mercury, Venus, or Jupiter is situated in the <term>House of Gains</term> <note>the eleventh house</note>, it ensures the victory of the Commander-in-Chief. The same applies to the tenth house. If those who are dear to the leader are in the Ascendant at the start of the march, O Goddess, it causes the defeat of the enemy's horeds. If Saturn <note>original: Arka-suta; "son of the Sun"</note> is in the lunar mansion of Ashlesha, or if the Sun is in the fourth house <note>original: Rasatala; the "underworld" or bottom of the chart</note>, the enemy's army will not arrive, or the situation will be reversed for their leader. When the Moon is in combination with Mercury, Jupiter, and the Sun, one should point to the arrival or departure of the military camp. If these planets are in the Ascendant, one should not predict the arrival of the enemy. O Goddess, when the Moon, Saturn, and the Sun are in <term>Moveable signs</term> <note>original: Chara Lagna; Aries, Cancer, Libra, or Capricorn</note>...

When planets are situated in the <term>House of Gain</term> <note>original: Labha; the eleventh house, signifying profit and fulfillment</note>, O fair-complexioned one <note>original: Varavarnini; an epithet for the Goddess Parvati, indicating this is a dialogue with Shiva</note>, or if Saturn and Jupiter are positioned in a <term>Fixed Sign</term> <note>original: Sthira-lagna; signs like Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, or Aquarius that represent stability</note> in the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>original: Lagna</note> or the seventh house, one should declare that there will be no arrival. 

If the lord of the third house is situated in the <term>House of Wealth</term> <note>original: Dhana-samstho; the second house</note>, and the placement is fierce, then, O dear one, you should predict the quick arrival of the traveler. For men born under the signs of Aries, Leo, or Sagittarius <note>original: Turaga; literally "horse," referring to the Archer/Centaur sign</note>, if the Sun and Saturn are present, or if there is death or retrograde motion <note>original: Vakra</note> as contemplated, heat or distress will come from the riverbanks or the ground. 

When the <term>benefic planets</term> <note>original: Saumya</note> reach the "son" <note>likely the fifth house</note>, the birth of a son should be predicted through Jupiter. In a fixed ascendant, or when the ascendant itself is "sleeping" <note>original: Svapa; a technical state where a sign is considered inactive</note>, one should declare the truth of a person wounded or called by their own kin. 

In the <term>movable portion</term> <note>original: Chara-amshaka</note> of a sign, there is service or departure from that place. In a <term>dual-natured sign</term> <note>original: Dvisvabhava; Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces</note>, the object is nearby. If wealth is stolen or hidden in the ground, and benefic planets are in the <term>Angles</term> <note>original: Kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> or the <term>Houses of Increase</term> <note>original: Upachaya; the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</note>, or in the second house, the seeker will obtain wealth like a king; there is no doubt about this. 

Even if a full Moon has moved into Taurus or is in its sign of debilitation, wealth is obtained from others, or one receives a profit associated with auspiciousness. O fair-complexioned one, based on the Lord of the Ascendant, one should predict destruction caused by malefic planets. 

O Goddess, the lord of the ascendant is the planet who rules that sign. The science of knowing the time of his <term>Planetary Period</term> <note>original: Dasha; a system used to predict when specific karmic influences will manifest</note> was spoken by the sages <note>original: Shakya-satyayah; possibly referring to Satyacharya, a famous ancient astrologer</note>. 

Through the Sun, the half-year <term>solstice</term> <note>original: Ayana</note> is known; through the Moon, the days and the stars. Through Mercury, the months are known, and through Venus, the fortnights <note>original: Paksha</note> are proclaimed. Through Saturn, the years are known. This is the traditional method for all planets. 

Henceforth, I shall describe the chapter named **Miscellaneous** <note>original: Prakirnakhya</note> in brief. 

When Saturn is in the Ascendant or its seventh house, and is beheld by the Moon, tell the seeker, O dear one, that a thief or a woman with white clothes and various praises [is involved]. Red marks and gold are seen when the Moon is in the Ascendant. If a benefic planet is moving through the sky, the story is one associated with <term>Righteousness</term> <note>original: Dharma</note>, even if it is only read or heard with strength.

Then, there is a meeting with relatives and the attainment of wealth. ### The Calculation of Three Outcomes

One must combine the day of the <term>Solar Transit</term> <note>original: Saṅkrānti; the moment the Sun moves into a new zodiac sign</note> with the specific weekday. Multiply this by the <term>Rudra</term> number <note>The number 11</note> and divide the total by three. From the remainder, one should declare whether the result is good or bad:

*   If the **remainder is one**: There will be great prosperity and growth.
*   If the **remainder is two**: The result will be mediocre or middling.
*   If there is **no remainder** (zero): One must predict loss.

This should be contemplated every month. 

### On the Recovery of Stolen Goods

O Parvati, by relying on the vowels and the syllables of a name, and by calculating the sequence of those letters, one can determine the arrival of a group. When the syllables are purified through multiplication, one should again indicate the divisions of the sounds. In the matter of the "well-being of thieves" <note>i.e., whether the stolen items or the thieves themselves will be found</note>, the Seven-faced One <note>original: Saptarānane; an epithet for Shiva</note> declares: 

To establish the location of thieves, take the sequence of numbers from the desired syllables. Taking three syllables together, the wise should multiply each one. By knowing the sequence of the letters, the determination of the month is made. Double the result and divide; the remainders point to the months. By repeatedly combining and dividing until the final portion is given, the exact months become clear.

### On the Birth of a Child

O fair-complexioned one <note>original: Varavarnini; an epithet for Parvati</note>, I have spoken the truth regarding the womb; it has not been hidden from you. When a question is asked, if the syllables of the name are **odd-numbered**, a son will be born from that womb. If the syllables are **even** (second or fourth), they are the generators of a daughter. This is also determined by the nasal sounds in the name...

### Determining the Sex of a Child

One should combine the number of the <term>Lunar Day</term> <note>original: Tithi</note> and the number representing the planet presiding over the hour. To this sum, add the numerical value of the <term>Limb of the Day</term> <note>original: ahorāṅga; likely referring to the specific portion of the day or the astrological sign rising at the time of the query</note>. 

Divide this total by **seven**. 

*   If the **remainder is even**: A girl shall be born. <note>original: kanyā syād</note>
*   If the **remainder is odd**: One should declare that a son will be born. <note>original: putra mādiśet</note>

This calculation should be applied to questions concerning a woman who is currently pregnant. <note>original: garbhasthāyāḥ striyo</note>

### The Method of Dividing by Seven and Three

In the matter of a formal inquiry, one should apply the measurement of the letters of the mother’s name. Multiply the sum by the appropriate factors and divide by seven. From the resulting quotient, if the remainder is one or more, the outcome is determined by the movement toward the earth or the netherworld.

Alternatively, one should perform the calculation by dividing by **three**:

1.  **If one remains**: The result indicates a portion of success or a high-quality outcome. 
2.  **If two remain**: The result is of a "middle" or mediocre fruit.
3.  **If nothing remains (zero)**: The outcome belongs to a "low family" <note>original: nīcakule; metaphorically meaning a poor, unsuccessful, or base result</note>. 

Of this, there is no doubt; this is the truth. 

### Analysis of the Name and Vowels

In all cases where one seeks to know the outcome, one must carefully consider the letters in the names of the woman and the man. One must calculate based on the <term>vowels</term> <note>original: svaraje</note> and the <term>varna</term> <note>original: varṇāḥ; the specific sounds or letters making up the name</note>. 

If the value of the name, when divided by three, leaves no remainder, it is influenced by the weight of the "Teacher" <note>original: Guru; referring to the planet Jupiter</note>. If the first value is related to the Sun, and the second to a woman, then one should speak accordingly. One must examine whether the name contains an even or odd number of syllables.

### Calculation of the Birth Timing

Regarding the "yoga" <note>astrological combination</note> in the right hand, one should perform the letters' calculation through the sequence of the elements of knowledge. The wise practitioner should slowly determine the time of birth from this. 

One should consider the counts of three, seven, and ten months. Count the number of days from the day of the transit and apply them here. Then, consider the month of birth, even up to the eleventh month. From that point forward, one should calculate the number of days and indicate the time of birth.

### Divination Through Dreams and Propitiation

Through the telling of a dream, or even without it, by the grace of Lord Nala <note>This may refer to a specific deity or a legendary figure associated with gambling and divination</note> in the sign of Aries, it is spoken thus: "O Beautiful One! There is a sorrow of twenty-five in all directions." From there, during the first seven days, one should observe the birth month. If the goal is attained, she will speak of it. By the inquiry alone, one must not forget what is said. 

In this very manner, if one propitiates the desired deity, then on the desired day, O Goddess, an answer may sometimes be revealed. 

### The Life-Thread and Bodily Omens

In whichever part the **Life-Thread** <note>original: prāṇa sūtra; refers to the flow of vital breath or energy used to determine the life or death of a subject</note> is situated, there are three pillars. If it remains there, the patient shall live; otherwise, the practitioner <note>original: sādhaka</note> will surely face death. 

If the breath exits through the right foot, like a swan, that man shall undoubtedly live. This is the "Single Umbrella" consideration. When there is movement in the right side or the heart, these signs should be observed at the time of the query regarding all matters, O Beautiful One:

*   **Positive Omens:** If the person touches the breasts, the soles of the feet, the back, teeth, head, throat, or the "crooked nose." 
*   **Success:** If they touch the private parts, the thighs, or if a swelling appears on the hand, or if they make a curved gesture—these are auspicious. One should declare that the attainment of all wealth and goals shall follow.

If the path is filled with milk and one remains firm in their speech, then one should determine whether the person is going or staying. One should consider death or the success of the task by observing if they are playing or falling. If they wander into another territory, the mind becomes troubled.

### On Victory and Defeat

**The Goddess said:** O Blessed Lord! I wish to hear about victory and defeat. How is this known, O God? Tell me this name, O Great Lord <note>original: Maheśvara</note>.

**The Lord said:** Listen, O Goddess, to the reality as it is known by Me. For the sake of a journey or a battle, one should know the "Rising of the Breath" <note>original: svodaya; referring to Swara Yoga, the science of breathing through the left or right nostril</note>. Without knowing this, how can one know the strength of the enemy?

# The Construction of the Hundred-Step Diagram

Having heard this, O Mistress, and knowing the time, let it be understood through the calculations of the masses. It is proclaimed that one should observe the nine steps each. O Beautiful Goddess <note>original: surasundari; an epithet for the Goddess, likely Parvati</note>, the **Zodiac Signs** <term>Rashi<note>The twelve signs of the zodiac used in Vedic astrology.</note></term> from Aries to Pisces, and the **Lunar Mansions** <term>Nakshatras<note>The 27 or 28 segments of the ecliptic; in this system, they are correlated with phonetic sounds.</note></term> starting with Ashwini, are spoken of in their proper order.

For the purpose of knowing the relationship between a person's name and the stars, this diagram is revealed. It is called the **Hundred-Step Diagram** <term>Shatapada Chakra<note>A 10x10 grid used to map names to astrological influences.</note></term>, which is regarded even above the knowledge of the Vedas. 

### Instructions for the Grid

O Beloved, one should first take the initial letter of the person's name. In the case of a conjunct consonant, take the first half combined with its vowel sound. Then, one should draw the cells: place ten cells horizontally and ten cells vertically. In this way, there are exactly one hundred cells for the knower of the stars. 

O Queen of the Devas, beginning from the first cell in the eastern direction, place them in order... <note>The text here describes a complex numerical sequence for filling the 10x10 grid, involving the numbers seven, two, and eight</note>. In the Western direction, the letters should be placed in the rows according to the prescribed sequence, O Beloved. 

### Phonetic Mapping

The vowels **A**, **I**, **U**, and **E** <note>original: akāra, ikāra, ukāra, ekāra</note> are the lords. One should grasp all these letters starting with 'A' combined with the vowels. O Queen of the Devas, write these letters in sequence, placing the syllables at the beginning and the end.

</margin>

There, the letters... <note>The text lists specific Sanskrit consonants to be placed in the grid cells</note>. In this way, within the hundred cells, the names are joined with their respective vowels. All these are to be known as "shining" or "burnt" depending on their position. According to the order of the stars mentioned, the letters are placed in the hundred-square grid.

<margin>

</margin>

The four vowels **A**, **I**, **U**, and **E** are placed in the position of the star **Kruttika** <note>The third Nakshatra, associated with fire and the Pleiades</note>. Similarly, O Beautiful One, **Rohini** <note>The fourth Nakshatra, considered very auspicious</note> is situated there... the sounds *Ka*, *Ko*, *Ha*, *Ra* are surrounded by... O Fair-Faced One, this applies to the evening as well as the day.

### Mapping the Celestial Grid

This is the diagram known as the **Hundred-Step Diagram** <term>Shatapada Chakra<note>A 100-cell grid used for divination based on the phonetics of a person's name.</note></term>. It is for the sake of true knowledge. This diagram, which belongs to the wise, must be constructed with great care. One should draw seven lines horizontally and seven lines vertically <note>The text mentions "seven lines" but earlier referred to "twenty" and "hundred"; this likely refers to the internal subdivisions or a specific method of drawing the 10x10 grid</note> to create the cells. 

O Goddess, at the ends of the lines, one should write the letters starting from the Eastern direction in proper order. Place the letters situated in the steps in the cells. Above these letters, one should place the **Lunar Mansions** <term>Nakshatras<note>The 27 stars used in Indian astrology.</note></term> starting with **Krittika** <term>Kritika<note>The Pleiades; in many ancient systems, the zodiacal count began with Krittika rather than Ashwini.</note></term>, ending with the final star.

### Phonetic Placements and Omens

In this same manner, place the vowels and the consonants joined with vowels in their respective positions. O Beloved, these should be placed in their own steps. 

1.  In the first quarter of the mansion **Krittika**, the letter **'Ca'** (च) is situated.
2.  Above that, a hundred malefic <term>Papatmaka<note>Literally "of a sinful or harmful nature"; refers to letters or positions that bring bad luck.</note></term> letters are placed.
3.  In the Western portion, the letter **'Ra'** (र) is located.
4.  The vowel **'E'** (ए) is situated at the center point <note>original: bindu</note> and spreads everywhere.

### Predictive Results

If the name falls upon these specific points, the following results shall occur:
- If placed in the excessive or outer cells: **Disease** <term>Roga<note>Illness or physical suffering.</note></term> shall manifest.
- If placed directly in front: **Destruction of one's work** <note>original: kritanasha</note>.
- If placed in the burnt or afflicted cells: **Failure** and **defeat** <note>original: bhanga</note> will occur within half a time period.

### The Results of Planetary Strikes

Regarding the results of the planetary positions and the "kicks" <term>Latta<note>A specific astrological affliction where a planet "kicks" or strikes a particular lunar mansion (Nakshatra) located at a specific distance from it, usually causing obstacles or misfortune.</note></term>: When the Sun <note>original: Ravi</note> is positioned in the first quarter of the Southeast direction <note>original: Agneya</note>, it influences the birth chart <term>Lagna<note>The ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth.</note></term>. In the twelfth year, if it is situated in the third quarter of the Deer's Head constellation <note>original: Mrigashirsha</note>, the "kick" or strike of the planet travels fifty <term>Yojanas<note>A traditional Indian measure of distance, roughly 8 to 9 miles.</note></term>, and specifically affects twenty-two units. 

The protection is sought from the deity Varuna <note>Lord of the cosmic waters</note>. When the Sun <note>original: Surya</note> is in the twelfth house, it brings certain results, but in the third, seventh, and sixth houses, it brings prosperity and beauty <note>original: Shri</note>. 

When the Sun is in the lead, the constellations are counted in reverse order. If the Moon <note>original: Shashi</note> and Sun are in the ninth house, it involves the Son of the Mist <note>original: Mihikasuta, a name for the shadow planet Rahu</note>. 

The Son of the Moon <note>original: Somaputra, referring to the planet Mercury</note> in the seventh house, and Venus <note>original: Shukra</note> in the fifth house, produce specific effects. However, there is destruction of the priestly class <note>original: vipra-nāśa</note> if Mars <note>original: Kuja</note>, Rahu <note>the north node of the moon</note>, and Saturn <note>original: Shanishvara</note> are poorly placed. This leads to the threat of death and total loss. 

If a powerful planetary combination <note>original: Yogi</note> is present, one becomes influential; however, if Venus is afflicted, the person's work will be frustrated and their efforts will be in vain. If a child is born under these "strikes," the results of the **Planetary Kick** <term>Latta Pata Phalam<note>The specific predictive outcome or "fruit" resulting from a planet striking a sensitive point in the horoscope.</note></term> are as remembered by the sages.

### The Seven-Line Diagram for Marriage Calculations

Now hereafter, I shall fully describe what is called the **Shalaka Diagram** <term>Shalaka Chakra<note>A divinatory grid used in Muhurta (electional astrology) to identify "Vedha" or planetary obstructions that could harm a marriage.</note></term>. Within this circle known as the **Seven-Line Diagram** <term>Sapta Shalaka<note>Literally "Seven Spikes," referring to the seven horizontal and seven vertical lines that form the grid.</note></term>, one can specifically know the good and bad results. 

Seven lines should be drawn from East to West, and likewise, seven lines should be drawn from North to South. Upon these, one should place the **lunar mansions** <term>Nakshatras<note>The 27 (or 28, including the intercalary Abhijit) sectors of the sky the Moon passes through.</note></term> in their proper order. Starting from the East, through the sequence of the twenty-seven stars, one should recognize where the "piercing" <term>Vedha<note>A negative astrological influence where a planet "pierces" or aligns with a star on the grid, creating an affliction.</note></term> of a cruel planet occurs at the front or the side. The results regarding the increase or decrease of prosperity must always be understood correctly. 

O Goddess, let me describe the results of this alignment. When the solar orb moves through these positions, O beautiful one, if a **benefic planet** <note>original: Saumya-graha; usually referring to Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury</note> is attained, there is satisfaction. However, in the context of marriage, the effects are described here as follows:

*   If the Sun <note>original: Ravi</note> pierces the star, it leads to widowhood. 
*   If Mars <note>original: Bhauma</note> pierces, it leads to destruction and widowhood. 
*   The piercing of Mercury <note>original: Budha</note> has also been declared for those two. 
*   From the piercing of the **Teacher of the Gods** <note>original: Devacharya; referring to Jupiter</note>, one becomes eternally sorrowful. 
*   If Venus <note>original: Shukra</note> pierces, the woman may fall from her status or become a wanderer. 
*   Through the piercing of Saturn <note>original: Shanaishchara</note>, it is well-known that she becomes a prostitute <note>original: Ganika</note>. 

When the Moon touches the tip of a line and is joined by both benefic and malic planets, two specific yogas are proclaimed: the **Time of Great Death** <term>Kalamahamrityu<note>A highly inauspicious astrological combination suggesting extreme misfortune or death.</note></term>. In stable works, in marriage, in entering a new home, or in setting out on a journey, there will be doubt and danger. For a bride who is being married, one should therefore avoid all of these afflictions.

The seven planets starting with Mars are the lords of the stars, seven by seven. If the lords of the houses are situated in these stars, they create these faults. In all works such as those concerning wealth, all prosperity is achieved when the lord of the house is favorable, O Great God, for the benefit of all living beings. Here it is said: a favorable position is best for a man, while in the end, it provides protection from fear and enemies. Otherwise, it gives disease, grief, and fear of demons to embodied beings.

<summary>This page explains the construction and interpretation of the Sapta Shalaka Chakra, an astrological grid used to predict the success of a marriage based on whether planets "pierce" certain stars.</summary>

Thus is the Inquiry into the Compatibility <term>Melana<note>The ritualized process of comparing two horoscopes to determine the success of a marriage.</note></term> written according to the words of the sage **Gautama** <note>One of the legendary seven sages (Saptarishi) credited with foundational wisdom in law and astrology.</note>. --- Page 27 ---
<meta>This page continues the astrological technicalities from the previous section, moving from specific inquiries about the Ascendant and auspicious combinations (yogas) into the rules for the "Shalaka Chakra" (a lattice-like divination grid) and planetary obstructions. It concludes Chapter 4 of the Rudrayamala Tantra and begins a new dialogue regarding the supreme charts.</meta>

One should know the positions of the <term>Seven Sages<note>original: Saptarshi; the constellation of Ursa Major, used in Indian astronomy to track long-term chronological cycles.</note></term> and the planets: the Sun, the Moon, Venus, Jupiter, Mars, Mercury, Saturn, and Rahu. O Great-Blessed One, these are placed within the divisions of the grid. When Saturn and the Sun are positioned in their respective divisions, the Moon encounters an <term>obstruction<note>original: Vedha; an astrological concept where one planet 'pierces' or neutralizes the influence of another based on their alignment on a specific grid.</note></term>. 

When the Sun and Mars are mutually obstructed, Mars likewise blocks the Sun. In the same way, O Goddess, Mercury and Jupiter are mutual enemies. Venus and Rahu similarly create an obstruction for the Moon. Whether in a city, a village, or a particular direction, these alignments affect trade, merchandise, and weaponry. 

The <term>Shalaka Chakra<note>original: Shalaka Chakra; literally 'the lath or spoke wheel,' a diagram used to calculate 'Vedha' (obstruction) by drawing parallel and intersecting lines through the zodiac signs.</note></term> consists of seven lines arranged in a specific order. By following this sequence, one can determine success or failure. If one proceeds toward the East or North when the planets are balanced, or toward the South or West depending on the planetary signs, they may find favor. However, O Goddess, the one who ignores these signs out of delusion and crosses the forbidden directions will find no happiness. 

In the space between the South and the direction of Fire [Southeast], a "Sea Combination" occurs. O Goddess, this <term>Ocean Yoga<note>original: Samudra Yoga; a specific planetary configuration that suggests instability or 'drowning' in one's efforts.</note></term> is mutually restrictive. One should strictly avoid it. During this Ocean Yoga, marriages and departures for journeys should not take place; one who leaves during this time does not return to their home. 

By testing the positions from the beginning of the chart, the results I have described will manifest in this manner. O Great-Blessed One, I have explained this "Excellent Grid of Spikes" [Shalaka Chakra] in great detail, according to the division of the name and the moment.

### **Thus concludes the Fourth Chapter, named the Fifty-Seven Divisions of the Lord of Knowledge, within the Shri Rudrayamala.**

---

**The Diagrams are written here.**

**The Holy Goddess spoke:**
O Lord, I desire to hear in detail about those diagrams which are the most excellent among all charts you have revealed.

**The Lord spoke:**
I shall tell you in detail, O Goddess, about the "Lord of the Charts" [Chakreshvara], which is joined with every significant verse.

...the discernment of one's own true interests. Here, we consider whether this is the **Wheel of Spikes** <note>original: "shatakachakra" likely a variant of "shalakachakra"; a grid used for divination based on intersecting lines.</note> or something else, as revealed through knowledge in the text above. The description of that which is attached horizontally and vertically in ten lines each is known as the **Eighty-one Square Grid** <note>original: "ekashitipada"; a 9x9 grid frequently used in Hindu temple architecture (Vastu) and astrology.</note> according to its majesty. In those cells, the vowels should be placed; the vowels are repeated. Following that, the lunar mansions beginning with **Krttika** <note>the Pleiades</note> are placed in accordance with the threads of the eastern and other directions, numbering twenty in total, as well as the initial sounds of names. 

Then, within the grid, the seven planets and the zodiac signs are placed in sequence, followed by the lunar days starting with **Nanda** <note>Nanda refers to the 1st, 6th, and 11th days of the lunar fortnight.</note>. In this way, the wheel is completed in the form of an eighty-one square grid. One should know that it is illuminated by the vowels, lunar mansions, letters, lunar days, and planets. If a **malefic planet** <note>original: "krura"; literally "cruel," referring to planets like Saturn or Mars</note> strikes through an obstruction, it leads to destruction; however, a twofold obstruction can be auspicious. 

A single obstruction leads to loss or perhaps a lack of wealth. Through a double obstruction of a person's name by two malefic planets, one should predict death. If there is an obstruction by a **benefic planet** <note>original: "saumya"; literally "gentle," referring to planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> on the letters of a name, it leads to the destruction of poverty. One is successful when there are two or three obstructions by benefics. In the rite of **conception** <note>original: "adhana"; the first of the samskaras or life-cycle rituals</note> or other ceremonies, destruction occurs if there is a malefic obstruction. 

A malefic planet produces its fruit even when it is "set" <note>original: "astamite"; referring to a planet being too close to the sun to be visible</note>. When the letters of a name are "set" (inauspicious), it results in death, loss, and similar outcomes. It brings conflict between a wife and husband, grief, and the loss of cattle or servants. However, when the letters of the name are **"risen"** <note>original: "udite"; active or visible in the grid</note>, victory, friends, and wealth follow. One's desires will be fulfilled, and unexpected gains will occur. 

Regarding the **T-group of consonants** <note>original: "tavarga"; the consonants Ta, Tha, Da, Dha, Na</note> and others: wherever the Sun is situated, that group is considered "set" or inactive. The remaining groups are considered "risen." One's own group, vowel, zodiac sign, lunar day, and direction are important here. How one should proceed on a journey is determined by these three "threads." Whichever group is "risen" among the nine groups of months, and whichever direction one travels in, that vowel and zodiac sign are essential. The direction in which one's own group has "set" is considered the direction for travel—balancing the auspicious and inauspicious...

# Page 14

The **Yogini** <note>original: "yogini"; a class of female deities or spirits whose movements through the compass points govern the success of travel and rituals.</note> manifests for the purpose of listening; there are five distinct mansions known by name... the signs of **Taurus** <note>original: "vrishabha"</note> and **Cancer** <note>original: "karkata"</note>. The men and the asses are positioned in the four corners; the fifth, the ninth, and the twelfth follow them. In the same manner, the five lunar mansions beginning with **Magha** <note>the mansion of the "Mighty," located in Leo</note> are assigned names. These are associated with the **Jaya** lunar day <note>original: "jaya tithi"; the "Victorious" days, which are the 3rd, 8th, and 13th of the lunar month</note> and the western direction.

From the mansion of **Dhanistha** <note>the mansion of "Wealth," in Capricorn/Aquarius</note> to the end of the cycle, there are five mansions covering the signs of **Aquarius, Taurus, Pisces, and Aries**. These correspond with the **Rikta** lunar day <note>original: "rikta tithi"; the "Empty" days (4th, 9th, and 14th), usually considered inauspicious for new beginnings</note> and the numerical qualities of the Vedas and the Sun. From the position of the Sun, the movement toward the northern direction is determined.

### Regarding the Secondary Planets

The presence of **Secondary Planets** <note>original: "upagraha"; shadow points or mathematical points in the sky that exert malefic influence</note> is determined by the position of the Sun. Where the Sun is situated in **Aries** <note>original: "mesha"</note>, the fifth sign from it becomes the secondary planet named **Vishkambha** <note>meaning "Obstruction" or "Support"</note>. 

In the tenth and eighteenth positions from that point, and likewise in the twenty-first, is the one named **Ulka** <note>original: "ulka"; meaning "Meteor," considered a portent of sudden change or disaster</note>. 
*   In the twenty-second is the one named **Arka** <note>named after the Sun, but here a shadow point</note>.
*   In the twenty-third is **Kampanam** <note>meaning "Tremor" or "Shaking"</note>.
*   In the twenty-fourth is the one known as the **Ketu** of the enemy <note>original: "ketumala"; referring to the descending node of the moon, associated with karmic debts and obstacles</note>.

In this world, these influences circulate. This combination of the Sun and the secondary planets follows the cycle of days and nights, the lunar days, and the twenty-seven lunar mansions. Thus, their rising occurs in the sequence of the weekday and the lunar day. If an obstruction is formed during one’s birth, it leads to loss and destruction. 

This is the teaching of **Sahadeva** <note>the youngest of the five Pandava brothers in the Mahabharata, traditionally credited with great astrological wisdom</note>. In the calculation of days and nights, if a **malefic planet** <note>original: "krura-graha"; literally a "cruel" planet like Saturn or Mars</note> occupies a **malefic lunar day** and a **malefic lunar mansion**, the result is total and complete destruction; everything becomes inauspicious despite any other forceful or positive factors.

# The Eighty-One Square Grid and the Strength of Planets

When a **planet** <note>original: "graha"</note> is diminished in strength, the results it produces are similarly weakened. Regarding the calculation of the grid, the number of squares should be eighty-one <note>original: "ekāśītipada"; a 9x9 grid common in Vastu Shastra (architecture) and astrology for mapping influences onto a space or body</note>. These squares are arranged and categorized by direction. One must perform the calculation by subtracting the relevant sounds or **vowels** <note>original: "svara"</note>. By excluding the twelve classes of letters, the specific region or country influenced by the grid is revealed. In the first square, the phrase "Dhiyo Vave" is placed.

The total eighty-one squares contain the various categories. The letter 'A' is placed once again in the twelfth square, and each should be directed according to its truth. In the twenty-first, twenty-fifth, and sixtieth squares, as well as the seventy-first, the calculations involve adding hundreds. One who resides in these squares is considered the "enjoyer" or ruler of that region, O fair-faced one! 

### The Sixteen Phases and the Lunar Fortnights

The sixteen **digits or phases** <note>original: "kalā"; the sixteen divisions of the moon's cycle</note> are to be remembered, and the sixteen **vowels** <note>original: "svara"</note> are likewise known to be sixteen. From these, a "good vision" or auspicious outlook arises; otherwise, there is suffering in the body. Whether in a gross or subtle state, the vowels manifest through the **Bright Fortnight** <note>original: "śukla pakṣa"; the waxing moon</note> and the **Dark Fortnight** <note>original: "kṛṣṇa pakṣa"; the waning moon</note>. Their strength and form are proclaimed here according to their positions.

The sounds, the lineages, and the children produced therefrom, as well as the types of knowledge, are determined by these letters. <insert>The mansions of **Bharani** and **Bhadra**...</insert> Through the power of the **Lunar Mansions** <note>original: "nakṣatra"</note>, great results are obtained. The mansions of **Vishakha** and **Shravana** are situated at the ends. The "Bamboo Village" <note>original: "veṇu grāma"</note> as well as the mansions of **Raika**, **Swati**, and the auspicious **Ashvini** and **Bharani** are positioned here. **Revati** and the others are situated in the sixth and second positions. When the planet **Mercury** <note>original: "saumya"</note> rises, it influences the lower regions and the town or city associated with these letters.

It explains how the 16 Sanskrit vowels correspond to the 16 phases of the moon to determine the physical well-being and prosperity of a location.</summary>
