<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the phonetic-astrological grid (Svarodaya). While the previous page established the eighty-one square grid and the significance of the sixteen vowels, this section focuses on the specific mapping of individual vowels and sounds to zodiac signs (Rashis) and lunar mansions (Nakshatras) to predict "malefic" outcomes, particularly the destruction or abandonment of settlements (Gramabhramsha).</meta>

# Phonetic Associations and the Ruin of Settlements

The following associations determine the influence of the celestial bodies upon a location. When a malefic influence <note>original: "pāpa"; referring to a naturally "cruel" planet like Saturn or Mars, or a negative astrological transit</note> is positioned in the lunar mansion of **Punarvasu** <note>the 7th Nakshatra, "the two restorers of goods"</note>, it results in the **ruin of the village** <note>original: "grāmabhramśa"; literally the displacement or falling away of a settlement or community</note>. In this system, the sound **'Da'** is linked to the **Sun** in the sign of **Gemini** <note>original: "bhāno yugma"; Yugma is another name for the zodiac sign Mithuna</note>.

### Vowels and Zodiac Signs

The vowel **'O'** is mapped to the sign of **Pisces** <note>original: "mīna"</note>, and the mansion of **Shatabhisha** <note>original: "vāruṇaṃ"; refers to the Nakshatra ruled by the deity Varuna</note>. Similarly, when a malefic influence is present in **Aries** <note>original: "meṣa"</note> or **Pushya** <note>the 8th Nakshatra</note>, it brings about a "cruel passion." It is said that the sign of **Cancer** <note>original: "karkaṭa"</note> becomes the source of anger and sin when these alignments collide.

Those with a "sinful soul" <note>referring to a planet in a highly debilitated or malefic state</note> are positioned in the direction of the **Anuradha** mansion <note>original: "maitra"; the 17th Nakshatra ruled by Mitra</note>. The sign of **Leo** <note>original: "mṛgendra"; literally "king of beasts"</note> and the sign of **Capricorn** <note>original: "makara"</note> act as auspicious indicators if the alignment is favorable, but the presence of the mansion **Shravana** <note>the 22nd Nakshatra</note> in this context may bring the threat of thieves or outcasts.

### Further Phonetic Mappings

*   The vowel **'Yo'** is associated with the sign of **Sagittarius** <note>original: "cāpa"; meaning "the bow"</note>.
*   The vowel **'U'** is linked to the sign of **Libra** <note>original: "bhāgya"; often associated with the 7th house or Venusian influences</note>, which can suffer a loss of power.
*   The mansion **Rohini** <note>the 4th Nakshatra, "the red one"</note> is particularly sensitive to these phonetic impacts.

In the cycle of the **Ashvins** <note>the twin physicians of the gods, associated with the first Nakshatra, Ashvini</note> and the sign of **Aries**, the movement toward **Punarvasu** again signifies a sinful or malefic disposition. This relates to the "yoni" or source of the ancestors <note>original: "pitṛyona"; likely referring to the Nakshatra Magha, which is ruled by the Pitris or ancestors</note>. 

### The Influence on Regional Prosperity

In the sign of **Leo** or the sign of **Cancer**, one must observe the placement of the sound **'Ka'**. If the mansion **Chitra** <note>the 14th Nakshatra, "the bright one"</note> or the **Purva Bhadrapada** <note>original: "pūrvabha"</note> is occupied by a malefic, it results in suffering. 

The sound **'Ge'** is associated with **Gemini**, while the wind-aligned mansion **Svati** <note>original: "anila"; ruled by Vayu, the wind god</note> and the **Shatabhisha** mansion stand at the forefront of this calculation. If **Aries** is in the position of gain, it brings a king-like equality; however, the presence of **Ashlesha** <note>the 9th Nakshatra, "the clinger"</note> is considered a sinful alignment that affects the **Vishakha** <note>the 16th Nakshatra</note> region. 

In the sign of **Scorpio** <note>original: "vṛścika"</note>, the vowel **'Ri'** is significant. The mansion **Bharani** <note>the 2nd Nakshatra</note> and **Anuradha** <note>original: "mitrabhā"</note> are here combined with the influence of the serpent <note>original: "sārpa"; referring to Ashlesha</note>.

<column-break/>

When the **Ashvins** and the "serpent" <note>Ashlesha</note> interact with the sign of **Pisces** <note>original: "matsya"; the fish</note>, it creates a "white-wind" malefic influence that can destroy a kingdom's vitality once more. The king of beasts (Leo) and the sign of **Virgo** <note>original: "pauṣṭa"; associated here with the sign Kanya or specific lunar phases</note> are also affected by the vowel **'E'**, which acts as a factor for auspiciousness <note>original: "bhadra"</note> or its opposite.

The vowel **'I'** relates to the theft of royal power. When **Saturn** <note>original: "śani"; though the OCR reads "śome" (Moon), the context of "nihati" (destruction) often implies Shani in technical grids</note> strikes, even the most fertile ground <note>original: "vṛtṛṇāva"</note> can be lost. In the sign of **Scorpio** <note>original: "kīṭa"; literally "the insect/scorpion"</note>, the pursuit of wealth and the influence of **Ashlesha** lead to malefic results. The vowel **'Ai'** is placed in the square for the mansion **Ashvini** to determine the fruits of one's actions. Finally, **Shatabhisha** <note>original: "vāruṇa"</note> stays in the grid to mark the destruction of a village if it remains unconquered by auspicious planets.

<summary>This page details the mapping of specific Sanskrit vowels and consonants to zodiac signs and lunar mansions to predict malefic events, specifically the abandonment of villages and the loss of royal power. It illustrates the "Svarodaya" system where phonetics and astrology intersect to determine the fate of geographic regions.</summary>
<keywords>Svarodaya, Nakshatra, Punarvasu, Gramabhramsha, Zodiac, Aries, Gemini, Leo, Scorpio, Phonetic Astrology, Vowels in Astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of phonetic-astrological mapping. While the previous page focused on the layout of the grid and the vowels' impact on settlement stability, this page details specific alignments of consonants, vowels, and lunar mansions (Nakshatras) that predict the fate of individuals, nations, and their rulers. It introduces the "Phana" or serpent-hood configuration of the grid.</meta>

# The Alignment of Sounds and Celestial Mansions

<page-num>15</page-num>
<sig>Phonetic Sound 15</sig>

The positioning of sounds follows a precise celestial order. The sound associated with **Leo** <note>original: "siṃha"</note> is established in the direction of the **Shravana** mansion <note>the 22nd lunar mansion, associated with "hearing"</note> and the **Hasta** mansion <note>the 13th lunar mansion, "the hand"</note>, arranged toward the **Southeast** <note>original: "āgneye"</note>. Similarly, the sound for **Pisces** <note>original: "mīna"</note> is placed where the **Punarvasu** mansion <note>the 7th lunar mansion</note> resides.

### The Serpent Grid and Phonetic Forms

The "hoods" <note>original: "phaṇā"; referring to a serpent-shaped diagram used in Svarodaya astrology to map influences</note> are positioned in front. Within the mansions of **Aries** <note>original: "meṣa"</note> and **Taurus** <note>original: "vṛṣa"</note>, when the sun rises, it is considered auspicious. However, if **malefic influences** <note>original: "pāpa"; referring to planets like Saturn or Mars</note> enter the mansion of **Purvabhadrapada** <note>the 25th lunar mansion</note>, those who are wise understand it as a sign of impending destruction.

The forms of the sounds **'Ga'** and **'E'** are shaped like a bow, while others take the form of arrows <note>the text describes the visual geometry of the letters within the grid</note>. In the mansions of **Revati** <note>the 27th lunar mansion</note> and **Capricorn** <note>original: "makara"</note>, the **first vowel** <note>the sound 'A'</note> is assigned.

The vowel **'U'** is specifically linked to the **Rohini** mansion <note>the 4th lunar mansion</note> and the sign of **Pisces**. It is said that the breath of life <note>original: "prāṇa"</note> resides within these "hoods" of the phonetic serpent.

### The Five Critical Mansions of Influence

Astrological fate is determined by counting from the mansion of one's birth. The following points are vital for prediction:

1.  **Birth Mansion** <note>original: "janma-bha"; the 1st mansion</note>: Governs the individual's origin.
2.  **Action Mansion** <note>original: "karma-bha"; the 10th mansion</note>: Governs work and influence.
3.  **Collective Mansion** <note>original: "saṃghātika"; the 16th mansion</note>: Governs associations and community.
4.  **Destruction Mansion** <note>original: "vināśa"; the 18th mansion</note>: Governs death and the end of cycles.
5.  **Mental Mansion** <note>original: "mānasa"; the 25th mansion</note>: Governs the mind and internal state.

When malefic planets transit these positions, specific ruins follow. An affliction to the **Action Mansion** leads to failure in work; an affliction to the **Collective Mansion** leads to divisions and internal strife. If the **Destruction Mansion** is struck, sickness and death prevail. If the **Mental Mansion** is afflicted, it results in the loss of memory and psychological distress.

### Impact on the Realm

These celestial patterns do not merely affect individuals, but also the broader structures of society:
*   **The Caste or Group** <note>original: "jāti"</note>
*   **The Country or Region** <note>original: "deśa"</note>
*   **The Coronation or Inauguration** <note>original: "abhiṣeka"</note>

If the phonetic alignments are corrupted by "sinful" planetary crossings in the central regions, the consequences are dire: **the Kings are tormented** <note>original: "rājānaḥ paripīḍyante"</note> and the stability of the entire kingdom is shaken.

<summary>This page explains the "Serpent Grid" (Phana) mapping vowels and consonants to specific Nakshatras and Zodiac signs. It details how malefic transits through five critical mansions (Birth, Action, Collective, Destruction, and Mental) lead to the suffering of individuals, social groups, and the ruling monarchs.</summary>

<keywords>Nakshatra, Zodiac signs, Janmabha, Karmabha, Sanghatika, Vinasha, Manasa, Phana, Svarodaya, royal affliction</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of phonetic-astrological mapping. While the previous page focused on the "Phana" or serpent-hood configuration of the grid, this text shifts to the "Kurma-rupa" or Tortoise-form arrangement. It concludes the fifth chapter of the Punayarnava Tantra, focusing on the "Wheel of Service" (Seva-chakra) used to determine the relationship between a master and a subordinate, and begins a new dialogue on the nature of life-breath (Svara).</meta>

# The Configuration of the Tortoise and the Wheel of Service

The residents are categorized based on their dependency, starting with the mansion of **Krittika** <note>the 3rd lunar mansion, associated with fire</note> and proceeding in groups of three. These are mapped across the regions, including the lands of **Panchala** <note>a historical region in northern India</note>, ensuring the proper placement of all those who have entered these domains.

The various signs and their origins are distinguished: the sign of **Leo** <note>original: "siṃha"</note> relates to the local lands, while those associated with the horse-form <note>original: "hayerūpo"; likely referring to Sagittarius or specific phonetic clusters</note> are placed under the influence of **Varuna** <note>the deity of the West and the cosmic waters</note>. 

### The Manifestation of the Tortoise Form

The triple-metered and five-fold divisions are brought forth through the **Gayatri** <note>a sacred Vedic meter of 24 syllables, here used as a mathematical or structural guide</note>. When these five divisions are combined, the **Tortoise Form** <note>original: "kūrmarūpa"; a traditional diagram where the map of India or a site plan is overlaid on the body of a cosmic tortoise</note> is revealed. This form, associated with **Mahadevishnu** <note>a composite form of the Great God Shiva and Vishnu</note>, bestows auspicious results when understood correctly.

The anatomy of this cosmic tortoise maps onto the geography of the world:
*   The **Heart** <note>original: "hṛdaye"</note> corresponds to the **Middle Country** <note>original: "madhyadeśā"; central India</note>.
*   The **Face** <note>original: "vadane"</note> is positioned in the **Eastern Region**.
*   The **Tail** <note>original: "pucchaṃ"</note> is located in the **West**.
*   The **Flanks** <note>original: "pārśvau"</note> of the tortoise are known as the **North** and the **South**.

The destiny of a place is told through this body: the presence of a **benefic planet** <note>original: "śubhagraha"; such as Jupiter or Venus</note> in these positions indicates prosperity, while the alignment of a **malefic planet** <note>original: "pāpagrah"</note> indicates misfortune.

### The Wheel of Service and the Eighty-One Squares

O Goddess, I shall now clearly explain the **Wheel of Service** <note>original: "sevācakra"; used to predict the success of a servant, practitioner, or seeker in relation to a master or deity</note>. By merely knowing this, a man can achieve whatever he intensely desires. 

In this system, the phonetic sounds are distributed across the body of the figure:
*   **Seven** sounds are placed on the **Head** <note>original: "mūrdhni"</note>.
*   **Seven** on the **Back** <note>original: "pṛṣṭhe"</note>.
*   **Seven** across the **Brow and Face** <note>original: "bhruvānane"</note>.
*   **Seven** in the **Heart**.
*   The remaining sounds are placed at the **Feet** <note>original: "pādayo"</note>.

The results of these placements for a seeker are certain:
1.  If the sound associated with one’s master falls on the **Head**, the seeker’s position will be stable and they will surely obtain a son <note>historically, this implies the continuity of one's lineage or legacy</note>.
2.  If it falls on the **Back**, profit and gain will be realized after **six months**.
3.  If it resides in the **Heart**, the seeker will obtain everything they desire.
4.  If it falls at the **Feet**, the seeker will experience constant wandering and **suffering** <note>original: "kleśo"</note>; of this, there is no doubt.

This system is known as the **Eighty-One Square Grid** <note>original: "ekāśīti pada"; a 9x9 grid common in Vastu Shastra and Tantric divination</note>, which also defines the Wheel of Service.

**Thus ends the fifth chapter, titled "The Determination of the Eighty-One Squares," in the glorious Punayarnava Tantra.**

***

### The Discourse on the Breath of Life

**The Goddess said:**
O Blessed Lord, God of Gods, Revealer of all signs! I desire to understand the hidden meaning of life and the secret knowledge of the **Lord of Svara** <note>original: "svareśvara"; refers to the science of phonetic breath and its mastery</note>.

<summary>This page concludes the fifth chapter of the Punayarnava Tantra, detailing the "Kurma Chakra" (Tortoise Wheel) and the "Seva Chakra" (Wheel of Service) for predicting success and geographical fortune based on an 81-square phonetic grid. It marks the transition to a new discourse on Svara (the science of breath/sound).</summary>

-> At the time of the Sun's transition <note>original: "saṃkrāntikāle"</note>, when the vital breath <note>original: "vayu"</note> moves through the right channel <note>original: "dakṣiṇanāḍi"; known as the Pingala or solar channel</note>, one should take refuge in the supreme wind that has begun its movement at that time. On the second day, O Goddess, the wind of the Lord of Seasons <note>original: "rtupate"</note> flows; for men, the body of the moon <note>original: "somahakāyo"</note> is thus freed from imbalances <note>original: "dosa"</note>. This continues until the twenty-fifth day, and finally, it proceeds in a sequence of single increments until it is joined with the thirtieth. When this period is fully completed, that indeed constitutes one year <note>original: "vatsara"</note> in sequence. <-

The position of the months and the days is established through the six-fold divisions. There are five "lotus-blossom" <note>original: "puṣkarāḥ"</note> periods; O fair-faced one, life is half-measured from the day of the radiant deity. I shall explain this specifically so that no doubt remains: when the Moon is situated in the name-mansion and the Sun <note>original: "ravi"</note> is in the fourteenth house, then the wise declare that time to be filled with happiness. The perfection of the union <note>original: "yoga"</note> is achieved through the breath of that moment. Therefore, the wise should thoroughly consider the commencement of any undertaking.

The page includes divinatory grids and references to the "Peacock-Mind" and "Tortoise" diagrams used for astrological calculations.</summary>

<meta>This page follows the introductory dialogue between Shiva and Parvati regarding the Wheel of Time (Kalachakra). It transitions from the theoretical framework of the previous page into the specific calculations of longevity based on the "Svara" or the flow of breath through the left and right nostrils. The text describes how a fixed flow in one channel over extended periods predicts the remaining time of a person's life.</meta>

When that Blessed Lord <note>original: "bhagavandevo"; referring to Shiva</note> spoke: Whether the breath is in the left or the right [nostril], the transition determines the division of a man's lifespan. In that crossing, the Lord [of Breath] resides in the center of the middle part. One should practice meditation <term>Samadhi; deep meditative absorption</term> at midnight, and with a concentrated mind, contemplate the truth and strength of the lifespan, even when it is hidden.

O Parvati, if the breath <term>Vayu; the vital air or wind</term> travels only in one path [nostril] from the start of the day, then that man has three years of life remaining, according to the rules of sequence. 

If it continues for ten days, he lives for a certain period; if for twenty nights, he lives for six months before dying, O Beloved. If it flows thus for twenty-five days during the summer, he lives for a certain time; if for twenty-two days, his life is two months. A man lives for one month if it flows for twenty-seven days. If the breath should flow for twenty days, he lives for half a month. Beyond that, if it flows for nineteen days, he lives for seven days. If it flows for thirty-five days or twenty-one days, [the life is short]. If it flows for twenty-two days, he has two days left; if for twenty-three days and the breath remains in that path, he lives for only one day. 

Such are the flows and their subsequent cessations. Regarding this knowledge of Time and of life, O Parvati, one must first understand the measurement. When the breath is measured at eight finger-widths <note>original: "astangula"; a traditional yogic measure of the distance breath travels outside the body</note>, the lifespan is determined there. At the moment of sunrise, if the breath is observed for seven months, the life is nearly complete. 

The life-force <term>Prana; the vital energy</term> is the gateway at dawn and at the rising of the sun. This is to be understood by the minds of those known as Yogis. Beyond this, I shall speak of the auspicious and inauspicious signs for Yogis in brief, O Goddess worshiped by the gods. 

The nine planets <term>Navagraha; the nine celestial influencers in Vedic astrology</term> are proclaimed: the Sun, the Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter <note>original: "somaja", literally 'born of the moon', usually referring to Mercury, but here appearing in a list of the nine influencers</note>, along with Ketu and Venus <note>original: "bhargava"</note>. These yield their fruits and determine the various divisions [of time and life].

<summary>This page details a precise Tantric system of "breath-prognostication," where the duration of uninterrupted airflow through a single nostril is used to calculate the exact remaining years, months, or days of a person's life. It concludes by introducing the role of the nine planets in determining these life-divisions.</summary>

<keywords>Svara Shastra, Science of Breath, Shiva-Parvati dialogue, longevity, Prana, Navagraha, breath measurement, prediction of death</keywords>

...[the results are] full, medium, or even lesser, O fair-faced one <note>original: "varānane"; an affectionate address to Parvati</note>. Six years are declared for the period <term>Dasha; a planetary time cycle</term> of the Sun <term>Surya</term>. 

Ten [years for the Moon] are thus proclaimed. Seven years are for Mars <term>Bhauma</term>. Eighteen years are known for Rahu <term>the North Node of the Moon</term>. Sixteen years are to be known for Jupiter <term>Guru</term>. Nineteen for Saturn <term>Shani</term>. Seventeen years are said for Mercury <term>Budha</term>. For Ketu <term>the South Node of the Moon</term>, seven years are known. Twenty years are declared for Venus <term>Bhargava</term>. These are the planetary sub-periods in their proper sequence. 

A maximum lifespan <term>Paramayu</term> of one hundred and twenty years is thus proclaimed. 

O beautiful one, ninety-seven divisions are completed [in this calculation]. Now, O fair-faced one, having applied these to the nine-fold grid and establishing them, one should consider the results of the periods <term>Dasha-phala</term> according to the nine deities. 

The maximum lifespan for those born in the first portion is thus proclaimed. In the third portion, it should be known as eighty years, and in the fourth, sixty years. One should multiply the measure of the maximum lifespan by the designated time-units <note>original: "natāḍikāḥ"; likely referring to 'nadis' or 'ghatikas', units of time</note>.

After identifying the birth star <term>Nakshatra; lunar mansion</term>, one should subtract the remainder by nine <note>a mathematical step to find the starting planetary period</note>. From the remaining portion of the maximum lifespan, the clear result is obtained. By that proportion, the specific sub-periods of each planet are to be calculated. The results of the period are derived and divided by the maximum lifespan. What is obtained is the period of Rahu, multiplied by its own years. From that, the maturation <term>Paka; the ripening of karma in a period</term> is determined. By multiplying the years by those figures, the results become days, then months, and from months, one should calculate the specific time. Thus, these periods should be known for each individual planet...

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of planetary periods and astrological calculations, moving from the general Vimshottari Dasha system (120-year cycle) into a specific method called "Viravedita." The text maintains the dialogue format common in Sanskrit Agamas, where the god Shiva instructs the goddess Parvati on the mysteries of time and fate.</meta>

These [results] are to be accomplished by the wise; fifteen [years] are equal for those living auspicious lives. Their specific details are explained in the *Viravedita* <note>A specific system of astrological calculation or a specific treatise</note>. The count of twenty-seven <note>original: "saptāvimshati"</note> is the number of the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term>. Starting from that, the calculation proceeds. Wherever the intercalary mansion <term>Abhijit Nakshatra</term> has gone, the calculation must be performed from the name-mansion for all undertakings. 

In the *Viravedita* system, a planet joined with only medium results is a causer of pain. By worshiping the gods and through the giving of gifts, one attains union. Through the wisdom of the sages, unnatural death <term>Apamrityu</term> is destroyed. The planets should be known by their position in the mansions, O Beloved <note>original: "Priye"; Shiva addressing Parvati</note>. Then, they should be known in sequence. At that time, there may be happiness, royal favor, and profit. From these, there also arises the possibility of calamities, loss of wealth, expenditure, or equality with women.

There is a combination <term>Yoga</term> for the acquisition of clothes and ornaments. In one's own country, there may be enmity or the birth of a daughter. Through the crushing of enemies and through conflicts with loved ones, or through the arising of "blood-bile" <note>original: "rakta-pitta"; an Ayurvedic term for bleeding disorders or inflammatory conditions</note>, the planets bring about union with women. 

Under the influence of Mars <term>Bhauma</term>, there is no assembly, but rather death or mere illness. There is loss of wealth and a decrease in dignity when traveling in one's own lands. If it is a royal period, there is honor and wealth, or there may be the arrival of gold, horses, and excellent gains. There may be dizziness caused by wind <term>Vata-bhrama</term> and great accumulation. In the period of Saturn, there is illness, conflict with sons and wife, and battle. There is loss of affection and loss of intellect, which occurs during travel to foreign lands. 

Through the combination of Saturn and Mars, there is a meeting with friends. There is great peace from the king. Under Ketu, there is the loss of sons and wealth, and conflict with enemies. There is fear, royal trouble, and quarrels with difficult women. Through the influence of Ketu, there is union with women, profit, and duty <term>Dharma</term>.

### The Arrangement of the Celestial Mansions

Continuously, [the mansions of] **Varuna** <term>Varuna: The deity of the cosmic waters, associated with the Nakshatra Shatabhisha</term>, **Sadhya** <note>Purva Ashadha</note>, and **Aikapada** <note>Purva Bhadrapada</note> constitute the first channel <term>Nadi: Literally 'pulse' or 'conduit,' here referring to a specific grouping of stars for divination</term>. Within that same channel are **Mrigashira** and **Tishya** <note>Pushya</note>. Through these parts, **Chitra**, **Maitra** <note>Anuradha</note>, and **Vasava** <note>Dhanistha</note> are also reckoned.

O Goddess <note>original: "Deveshi"; Shiva addressing Parvati</note>, the second channel is famously proclaimed to include **Ahirbudhnya** <note>Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Krittika**, **Rohini**, and the **Saumya** <note>Mrigashira</note> mansions. Furthermore, the **Pitrya** <note>Magha</note>, **Swati**, the **Vishvedevas** <note>Uttara Ashadha</note>, and the **Vaishnava** <note>Shravana</note> mansions are considered another group, O Beloved.

### Determination of Fortunes

Through the combination of these lunar mansions, the determination of auspicious and inauspicious outcomes is made. If a planet is situated in the mansion of a traveler <term>Yatrika-bha: The specific star under which a journey begins</term>, it may cause destruction there. From the beginning, one must observe which planets are stationed there, as even one planet can indicate a lack of wealth. In this system, one must calculate the various divisions and the remaining angles. Among these:

*   Through **Malefic Planets** <term>Papa-grahas: Planets like Mars or Saturn that are traditionally seen as challenging</term>, there will be failure or "breaking."
*   Through **Benefic Planets** <term>Saumya-grahas: Kind planets like Jupiter or Venus</term>, one should predict happiness.
*   By the **Sun** (Surya), there is fear and terror.
*   By **Rahu** <term>The North Node of the Moon, depicted as a dragon's head</term>, one should predict total failure.
*   By **Saturn** (Sauri), imprisonment is spoken of.
*   By **Mars** (Urja) <note>original: "Urja," literally "Vigor," a common epithet for the fiery planet Mars</note>, death may occur.
*   By the **Moon** (Chandra), there is an auspicious union.
*   By **Mercury** (Budha), one becomes a person skilled in the arts.
*   By **Jupiter** (Jiva), there is union with the righteous.
*   By **Venus** (Shukra), one is endowed with great intelligence.

### The Diagram of the Path (Pathachakra)

There, one obtains freedom from disease and gains honor, O Parvati. This **Diagram of the Path** (*Pathachakra*) is declared to be beneficial for those undertaking journeys. One should calculate the channel starting from the lunar mansion of one’s birth. Wherever a "cruel" <note>malefic</note> planet exerts pressure, one should apply the corresponding quality to that zodiac sign. 

One should predict a "piercing" <note>a mathematical or divinatory affliction</note> through the twenty-seven divisions. 

### The Hunter’s Diagram (Akhotaka Chakra)

Hereafter, I shall explain the supreme **Akhotaka Diagram** <term>Akhotaka Chakra: Likely derived from 'Akheta' (hunting); a diagram used to determine if one is the 'hunter' or the 'prey' in a specific situation</term>. Between the elements of the Moon and the [Sun?], those mansions which are embraced by malefic planets indicate the death of living beings. When the internal figures are joined with malefic influences, or the signs are combined with enemy planets, the intensity is tripled. 

O Gauri, all these totals should be multiplied. In these matters, the "strikes" <note>negative planetary hits</note> must be calculated; it is not merely the simple union of planets. Even in the combination of the "Doubtful Lotus," these move to the lower portion. This diagram—

### The Method of Determining Success

O Great Goddess <note>original: "Mahadevi"</note>, I shall now explain the method for producing reliable results. In this very manner, O counter of stars <term>Ganavika: A practitioner of astrological mathematics or Gananā</term>, I speak of the calculation involving the planets, the lunar days <term>Tithi: A lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term>, and the zodiac signs. 

When the remainder is divided <note>the text uses "dalite," implying a mathematical reduction or splitting</note> among the planets, the living beings influenced by the Sun’s position are determined by the remaining parts. When one part remains, a person may flee; the specific zodiac sign <term>Rashi: One of the twelve signs of the zodiac</term> present there must be examined with effort by the wise.

Now, O Ganavika, I shall describe another method in this same way. Combine the movement of the lunar day with the reduced form I have described. Divide that by the measures of time <note>original: "nadibhir," referring to Ghatis/Nadikas</note>, and whatever remains will reveal the speaker among the living beings in their respective quarters. To the wise, this reveals the auspicious or inauspicious nature of that day regarding the hunt <term>Akhetaka: A hunt or expedition; here used as a metaphor for pursuing a goal</term>.

### The Hunter’s Diagram

If there is a disturbance or a clash of forces, or a fall on the eighteenth [day or unit], or a shift in the birth-group <note>original: "janma-bhe-gane"</note>, the remaining results may still be beautiful, O Ganavika. This is the fruit of the **Hunter’s Diagram** <note>original: "Akhetake Chakram"</note>. It is also known as the **Diagram of Illusion** <note>original: "Maya Chakram"</note>. A master of the counting of time <note>original: "Ganatyayah"</note> should only begin an undertaking after first examining this Primordial Diagram.

In that diagram, the twelve zodiac signs are assigned. One should mark the Sun, the lunar days, the six seasons, and the mountains <note>these represent specific numerical values in Sanskrit poetry: Sun=12, Tithi=15, Seasons=6, Mountains=7</note>. Following this, write the eight directions. Then, write the vowels and the consonants in their proper sequence. The vowels—**A, I, U, Ri, Lri**—and the Visarga <note>the 'h' sound in Sanskrit</note> are described in the row of vowels.

### The Grid of Fifty

These letters act within a grid of fifty cells <term>Koshthaka: A square or cell in a divinatory grid</term>. There, the vowels and primary letters are arranged by those seeking the truth. By totaling the numbers associated with the names of the participants and dividing by seven, the remainders determine the "Rising of the Banner" and the subsequent sequence. 

Just as in the corruption of sounds <note>original: "apabhramsha"</note>, this calculation determines the strengths of the following entities. The sequence of numbers should be contemplated through these forms:

**The Sequence of the Eight Animal Totems:**
1. **The Banner** <note>original: "Dhvaja"</note>
2. **The Crow** <note>original: "Kaka"</note>
3. **The Donkey** <note>original: "Rasabha"</note>

7. **The Lion** <note>original: "Panchasya," literally 'Five-faced'</note>
8. **The Dog** <note>original: "Shvajana"</note>

One follows from the other, starting from the Banner...

### The Diagram of Gain and Success

Distinctions between male and female, as well as specific types of substances <note>original: "trina-jati," literally meaning types of grass/vegetation, but here referring to foundational categories</note>, are calculated through the process of division. This is combined and declared as the standard procedure. The remainder is then used to determine wealth and other outcomes through the seven-fold reverse movement.

This is the **Income Diagram** <term>Aya Chakra: A divinatory grid used to calculate prosperity or the 'income' of luck</term> that I have spoken of, which is difficult to obtain even for the solar deities. Now, the letters of the name <note>original: "namaksharani"</note> are shown for the sake of demonstrating victory and triumph over crushing forces.

| 14 | 11 | 2 | 12 | 15 | 6 | 4 | 3 | 17 | 8 | 5 |
| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| a | ā | i | ī | u | ū | e | ai | o | au | am |
| ka | kha | ga | gha | ṅa | ca | cha | ja | jha | ña | ṭa |
| ṭha | ḍa | ḍha | ṇa | ta | tha | da | dha | na | pa | pha |
| ba | bha | ma | ya | ra | la | va | śa | ṣa | sa | ha |

The calculation is performed by taking the numerical values assigned to the letters of a person's name and the vowels within that name separately. These should then be multiplied respectively by two and four. After combining these two sums, the total should be divided by seven.

The resulting remainder <term>Asheshanka: The remainder left after a division calculation</term> reveals the strength or weakness <note>original: "balabalam"</note> of the person. If the numbers are equal, the result is stable; if one is higher or lower, the outcome changes accordingly. In the field of conflict, one must observe whether the numbers are even or odd. 

In the case of odd numbers, there is certainly humility or submission, but if the remainder is higher and even, the other side prevails. For instance, if the remainders are even numbers, or specifically if the remainder is four, or in the third position, victory is attained. When one's own number is higher than the opponent's, or if both have odd numbers but one is significantly higher, that person becomes the victor. If both have even numbers, the one with the higher number is destined for victory.

O Goddess, this excellent diagram is the maker of certainty. 

### The Human-Form Diagram for Battle

Now, I shall describe the characteristics of entering a strike at the time of battle <term>Sangrama: War, battle, or violent encounter</term>. The **Human-Form Diagram** <term>Purushakriti Chakra: A mystical chart where astrological data is mapped onto a human figure</term> should be used to know the outcome of a man's endeavor. 

Having understood the human form, one must meditate upon it. Divide the body into eight parts where the stations of the stars <note>original: "udutah," referring to Nakshatras or constellations</note> are placed in order: 

1. The Head
2. The Neck
3. The Face
4. The Heart (assigned 5 units)
5. The Two Hands (assigned 8 units)
6. The Two Feet (assigned 8 units)

This must be contemplated carefully. Wherever the "Moon" <note>original: "mesa," likely a shorthand for the lunar position or Aries sign in this context</note> is situated, one should indicate the result for that specific limb. The measure of success or failure is then determined by the proportions of those parts, measured in finger-breadths <term>Angula: A traditional unit of measurement approximately the width of a finger</term>. Based on the number of planetary influences, the measure in fingers will be either increased or diminished.

# The Spear Diagram

Death may occur here if malefic <note>original: "papaih," referring to planets like Mars or Saturn that are traditionally seen as bringing harsh or difficult results</note> influences are present. Therefore, O Goddess, one should clearly inscribe [the Mansions] upon the triple spear. One who is expert in the rules should arrange the Mansions <term>Nakshatras: the 27 or 28 "Lunar Mansions" used in Indian astrology to divide the sky</term> in the proper sequence. In the middle of these, and in the gaps between them, one should sequentially place the auspicious [marks].

Similarly, in those intervals, twelve mansions are to be placed evenly. Then, the wise man should speak the results in order according to their positions on the spears. In those positions [one may find] pain, fear of illness, destruction, or quarrel; likewise, the loss of wealth, the destruction of livestock, or the loss of kinsmen. In the case of traveling abroad, [the results may be] enjoyment or [encounters with] kings, occurring in their respective order.

When neutral influences are present, one should instead predict the birth of children, the gaining of property, or the performance of sacred rituals. O Goddess, when the time of war is at hand, or if one is struck by disease, one should consider the results of this Spear Diagram. Indeed, the commencement of a battle should only occur after contemplating this Star Diagram, O lover of results.

One should arrange fourteen squares <term>kosthakas: the individual cells or boxes in a divinatory grid</term> in a horizontal layout. These correspond to the lineages of animals: the Goat, the Cat, the Tiger, the Cow, the Deer, the Horse, the Monkey, and the Elephant.

# Classifications of the Lunar Mansions

## The Three Temperaments

It is said by the poets and the Sage Vasistha that there are affinities between the Mansions. Just as there is natural affection for a husband, O Beloved, the following should be understood: the first Cow, the latter Cow <note>referring to the pairings of "Uttara" Mansions</note>, Pushya, the Deer-headed mansion, the Sons of Aditi <note>Punarvasu</note>, Anuradha <note>Maitra</note>, Swati <note>Anila</note>, and Shravana.

The Sages have taught that these are the favorites of the Lord. Likewise, the three "Uttara" mansions <term>the 'Latter' mansions: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</term>, as well as Rohini, are known as the **Human** <term>Manusha: the temperament associated with hard work and human affairs</term> group. Magha, Dhanishtha <note>Vasu</note>, and Shatabhisha <note>Varuna</note> belong to the other. Vishakha and the Vishvadevas <note>Uttara Ashadha</note> are considered the best among the **Demon** <term>Rakshasa: a temperament associated with intensity and power</term> group.

Affinity is highest between those of the same temperament. It is middling between a Human and a God <term>Deva: the temperament associated with piety and refinement</term>. The lowest affinity exists between a God and a Demon.

| | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Ashvini | Krittika | Ashlesha |
| Mrigashira | Purva Phalguni | Magha |
| Punarvasu | Purva Ashadha | Chitra |
| Pushya | Purva Bhadrapada | Vishakha |
| Anuradha | Uttara Phalguni | Jyeshtha |
| Swati | Uttara Ashadha | Mula |
| Shravana | Uttara Bhadrapada | Dhanishtha |
| Revati | Rohini | Shatabhisha |
| **Gods** | **Humans** | **Demons** |

Therefore, a Demon temperament and a Human woman [marriage] results in a human-like life, or perhaps the gain of wealth. When a God-temperament leads the way, there is no doubt that all struggles are overcome. The union of a Demon and a Human is considered equal by the wise, O Beloved. Therefore, these should be applied at all times.

The following seven mansions are the givers of auspicious results: Ashvini, Pushya, Mrigashira, Punarvasu, Anuradha, Swati, and Shravana. These are helpful in cases where something is lost, killed, or fallen.

Ardra, Mula, Shatabhisha <note>Varuna</note>, Swati, and the three "Purva" mansions are known as **Eye-less** <term>Netra-hina: "blind" stars that are considered inauspicious for finding lost items or starting journeys</term> at all times.

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of celestial and alphabetical influences found in the Kudyamala Tantra. While the previous page focused on the animal lineages (Yonis) of the Lunar Mansions (Nakshatras), this page transitions into "Varna-bala" (the power of phonemes), assigning groups of the Sanskrit alphabet to specific classes of beings and animals for use in martial and directional divination.</meta>

# The Power of Letters and Directions in the Kudyamala Tantra

Great obstacles are established in those directions, and similarly, it is said there is no sovereignty in the Southeast direction. The region of Kekaya <note>A historical kingdom in the northwest of the Indian subcontinent</note> is proclaimed to be auspicious, acting as a revealer of specific tasks.

Furthermore, the Lunar Mansions of **Krittika** <note>the Pleiades</note>, **Aryaman** <note>Uttara Phalguni</note>, **Indragni** <note>Vishakha</note>, **Vasu** <note>Dhanishtha</note>, and **Abhijit** <note>Vega</note>, when occurring in the month of Pausa, are declared to provide unfavorable results in the sky. On such a day, one should avoid the pursuit of profit or the application of wealth.

The groups of letters are categorized as follows:
- The **Ca-group** <note>c, ch, j, jh, ñ</note> belongs to the Gods.
- The **Ka-group** <note>k, kh, g, gh, ṅ</note> are proclaimed as the Demons.
- The **Ca-group** <note>likely a scribal error or variation for another group</note> are the Nagas <note>Serpent-spirits</note>, O Goddess.
- The **Ta-group** <note>ṭ, ṭh, ḍ, ḍh, ṇ</note> are born of the Gandharvas <note>Celestial musicians</note>.
- The **Ta-group** <note>t, th, d, dh, n</note> belong to the Sages.
- The **Pa-group** <note>p, ph, b, bh, m</note> belong to the Rakshasas <note>Demonic beings</note>.
- The **Ya-group** <note>y, r, l, v</note> also belong to the Nagas.
- The **Sha-group** <note>ś, ṣ, s, h</note> belong to the Humans.

In times of war, one should determine strength according to this sequence. The consideration of the **first letter of a name** is thus proclaimed. In this way, the power [of sounds] is declared in this Tantra, the *Kudyamala*. In times of battle, the outcome may be known through this wheel <note>original: "Chakra," a mystical diagram</note>.

Whether in the directions of Indra <note>East</note>, Yama <note>South</note>, the Rakshasa <note>Southwest</note>, Vata <note>Northwest</note>, Soma <note>North</note>, or Isha <note>Northeast</note>, the letters are arranged in order. The corners <note>intermediate directions</note> are situated within these corners. This is told to you: the one who is in their own direction is strong, as is the one who is in a friendly direction. When two are in their own directions, they are equal.

# The Omens of Birds and the Eightfold Grid

The directions starting with the friend <note>original: "Mitra", referring to the Sun or a specific directional deity</note> are marked, and then the birds: the **Vulture**, the **Hawk**, the **Owl**, and the **Brown Owl** <note>original: "Pingala"</note>—these are the seven—and also the **Fish-bird**, the **Hole-dweller**, the **Osprey**, and the **Lapwing** <note>original: "Govatsa", literally 'cow-calf', referring to a specific bird species in omenology</note>. These are the birds. In this sequence, one should understand the rulers of the **A-group** <note>the vowels</note>, the rulers of the **Ca-group** <note>the palatals: c, ch, j, jh, ñ</note>, and the **Sha-group** <note>the sibilants: ś, ṣ, s, h</note>. These should be placed within the rows of the eightfold grid, and they should be known in this very manner.

Where they are joined by **benefics**, there is benefit and well-being. Among the "cruel" ones at the sharp tips, if there are no benefics present, the outcome is dire. However, if there is a mix of malefic and non-malefic planets in the sharp sectors, one should predict "victory only."

When only benefics are situated there, the roots of victory and gain are established. In the "Face," the wise practitioner should recognize the conjunction through the presence of mixed influences. Between the strength and weakness of the benefic and malefic natures, one should extract the correct reasoning from any contradiction of signs.

This arrangement of the pots is made according to the order of the mantra. Know that death is found in the **Empty Pot** <note>original: "rikta," meaning a void or unlucky vessel</note>, while victory is found in the **Full Pot** <note>original: "pūrṇa"</note> without a doubt. If the Sun is in the empty position, victory may still come if the Full Pot is indicated. Entering the auspicious-bestowing pot, one conquers the three groups in the wheel.

### Thus ends the Seventh Chapter, titled "The Authority of the Wheels," in the glorious Tantra of the Ocean of Victory in War.

# The Configuration of the Fortress Wheel

Regarding the **Lunar Mansions** <term>Nakshatras: The 27 segments of the ecliptic through which the Moon passes</term>, in the eighth position <note>likely referring to the eighth house or a specific directional division</note> is **Rohini** <note>The "Red One," a mansion associated with growth</note>. In the same way, **Purva Phalguni** <note>The "First Reddish One"</note>, and **Abhijit** <note>The "Victorious" intercalary mansion</note> are assigned. Then **Varuni** <note>another name for Shatabhisha, ruled by the god Varuna</note>, **Ashwini**, and **Ardra** are placed. These three are situated in the seven directions <note>original: "tri-sapta-dik"</note> along with the lunar mansion **Pitr** <note>Magha</note>. So too are **Vishakha**, **Maitra** <note>Anuradha</note>, and **Hari** <note>Shravana</note> placed within the structure.

### The Alignment of the Gates

Through this arrangement, **Pisces** <note>Meena</note> is situated at the **Eastern Gate**. **Cancer**, **Gemini**, and **Leo** are situated at the **Northern Gate**. **Libra**, **Scorpio**, and **Virgo** are supported at the **Western Gate**. **Capricorn** and the remaining signs <note>Aquarius and Aries</note> are similarly situated at the **Southern Gate**.

The three **Channels** <term>Nadi: Psychic or astrological conduits within the diagram</term> are known as the **Outer**, **Middle**, and **Inner** <note>original: "Garbha," literally meaning womb or core</note>. Through the power of these channels, the movement of time and energy is observed. One should observe the prosperity or depletion of the fort through these divisions.

### Results of Planetary Movement

Success <term>Siddhi: The accomplishment or victory of the fort's defense</term> is determined by the arrival of the planets. If a **Benefic Planet** <note>original: "shobhana-graha," such as Jupiter or Venus</note> enters, it is auspicious. If **Malefic Planets** <term>Krura: "Cruel" planets like Mars or Saturn</term> enter, they bring the achievement of their own cruel ends. If the planets are **Moderate** <note>of mixed nature</note>, the result is moderate or mixed. 

In the **Fortress Wheel** <term>Kotta Chakra: A diagram used to predict the fate of a besieged castle</term>, one should always look for balance. If a planet is on the **Exterior**, there is always success for the attacker; if in the **Entrance**, it serves a specific purpose; if in the **Middle**, it serves the internal needs of the fort. **O Dear One**, success for the fort itself is rarely seen when "Cruel Spirits" <note>malefic planetary influences</note> are staring at it.

### The Pillars of Stability

**O Queen of the Gods**, if **Auspicious Planets** stay within the **Pillars** <term>Stambha: The supporting internal points of the astrological diagram</term>, that fort cannot be destroyed. Even if the signs, the groups, and the lunar mansions appear unfavorable, one should know there is no fear for that fort, **O Beautiful-Faced One**.

However, if the **Sun**, **Rahu** <term>The shadow planet of the North Node</term>, **Saturn**, and **Mars** <note>original: "Bhumiputra," literally Son of the Earth</note> are situated within the **Pillars**, they become the destroyers of the assembly. Even if the fort was built with great effort and is well-protected, it will surely succumb to the "Anger of the Planets," **O Beautiful One**.

When **Malefic Planets** are situated on the **Enclosure Walls** <note>original: "Prakara"</note>, it is said by the Lord of the City that the fort will be pierced or broken. If a "Piercing Planet" is on the edge of the wall, then even the most hidden interior of the fort is at risk. There, even the internal machinery <note>original: "Yantra"</note> or the very core itself will be disturbed. Whether from the beginning, or through the influence of **Venus** <note>Bhrigu</note> or **Mercury** <note>Pragya</note>, **O Dear One**, a "Yoga of Death" <note>a fatal astrological combination</note> may occur.

It emphasizes that malefic planets (Sun, Rahu, Saturn, Mars) in the "pillars" signify total destruction, while benefics ensure invincibility.</summary>

# The Analysis of the Fortress Wheel

## Determining Auspicious Outcomes

In this system, if there is a perceived "pillar" <note>original: "stambham," referring to a structural or astrological obstruction</note> in the movement, what then is the result for the interior of the fort? Through the observation of the rising signs <term>Udaya: The ascent of a zodiac sign or planet on the horizon</term>, those skilled in the **Fortress Wheel** <term>Kotachakra: A specialized astrological chart for military divination</term> can determine if that place is auspicious. This is determined through the "piercing of the rods" <note>original: "shalaka bhedane," a technique of drawing lines across a grid to see which celestial bodies are intersected</note> and by using grids such as the **Eighty-One Square Layout** <term>Ekashitipada: A standard 9x9 architectural and astrological grid</term> and other related wheels like the Pillar Wheel.

By applying these methods, one understands the happiness or distress that may befall the inhabitants. Having mastered these sciences, one should begin the calculations for the Fortress Wheel. Without this knowledge, a practitioner is like one who is blind.

# The Fall of the Fortress

## Signs of Defeat for the King and the People

In this manner, the fort is analyzed. The defeat <term>Bhanga: The literal "breaking" or fall of a fortified position</term> of the king, the populace, or a weakened ruler is determined as follows: if Saturn <note>original: "Shani"</note> acts as a spike <note>original: "shanku," referring to an astrological obstruction or gnomon</note> within the fort's chart, or if the "Ancestors" <note>original: "Pitaro," referring to the lunar mansion Magha</note> enter a specific direction, then a fault or affliction <note>original: "dosha"</note> arises in that direction for the Lord of the Fort. If the Lord of the Stars <note>The Moon</note> is in a state of wrath, distress will follow. In such a case, there is a specific method for the enemy of the Lord of the Fort.

Through the power of horses and the Saurashtra people, one is overwhelmed. In the Northeast <note>original: "Ishaniye"</note> direction, there resides the "womb" <note>original: "garbhakam," likely a central or hidden point of vulnerability</note>. If the first planet is moving in **retrograde** <term>Vakraga: The apparent backward motion of a planet, often signaling intensified or malefic effects in military astrology</term>, there is a defect in the location. The siege <note>original: "aparodha"</note> is then rendered ineffective or "actionless" in the enemy's village by the waters. On that day, the son shall depart.

## Planetary Positions and Success in Battle

One must understand the following Lunar Mansions <term>Nakshatra: The 27 sectors of the sky used in Vedic astrology</term> when calculating the fort’s fate:
*   **Krittika** (associated with Agni/Fire)
*   **Magha** (associated with the Ancestors)
*   **Dhanistha** (associated with the Vasus)

These are to be known through the math of the "Varneshas" <note>lords of the colors or letters</note>. When a planet exits or enters these stations, the external and internal states of the city are determined. 

On **Tuesday** <note>original: "Bhaumavara," the day of Mars</note>, success is declared. In the "Mars-Fire-Battle" <note>original: "Bhauma-agni-samare"</note>, success is achieved through the power of the presiding malefic deities. If two malefic planets are positioned in the chart, it is a sign of great intensity. Particularly in the North, if the retrograde motion occurs, the fort <unclear>m-something</unclear> will surely break and the walls will fall. 

When Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> is in retrograde, a breaking [of the fort] will occur. This is a fixed certainty; defeat is the fate decreed by the gods. Similarly, if **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> is in retrograde, the breaking of the fort will occur from that specific direction. If [a planet] is situated there, it is not known to be auspicious for those with sons. 

Even if Venus <note>original: "Sita"</note> is retrograde, it does not have the power to properly cause the breach of the fort. Venus and Jupiter <note>original: "Ruja-Sita," possibly referring to the white/bright planets</note>, when not in retrograde, are bringers of auspiciousness during a journey or military expedition.

# The Internal Dynamics of the Fortress Wheel

## The Alignment of Lunar Mansions

The designated sequence of the lunar mansions <note>original: "bhani," referring to the Nakshatras</note> is inscribed in a specific order, beginning with those that are bright and cooling. O Sovereign Queen <note>original: "Deveshi"; the text addresses a high-ranking student or is part of a dialogue with a deity</note>, if the "Moon-Enemy" <note>likely referring to Rahu or a malefic planet</note> has afflicted the physical form of the fortress, one must observe the passage of the mansions with a single-minded focus. In all activities, one should record the mansions in their proper order in the charts to determine the results for the King.

Following this, the calculation of the strength of the qualities <note>original: "guna"</note> is performed. If there is a displacement or loss of the established position, it results from the influence of the five elements. For one who holds the weight of the kingdom <note>original: "raja-shir-dharin," the ruler</note>, there should be a delight in righteousness and the company of the wise. However, when the rising signs are afflicted, there is a forewarning of the loss of a son, or suffering caused by intense fever and distress.

## Malefic and Benefic Influences on the Siege

The calculation of divisions is made when the Sun and Moon are afflicted by disease or distress. One must understand the movement of the "Eight Positions" <note>referring to the eight directions of the Kotta Chakra</note> which determine the falling or the stability of the fort. Through the aspects of the Great Teacher <note>original: "Guru," the planet Jupiter</note> and the Messenger <note>original: "Budha," the planet Mercury</note>, wisdom is attained. Mercury and Jupiter are said to be the "wise ones" and the "movers of the wind."

These planets are known as those that "enter the womb" <term>Garbhannaga: Planets that occupy the innermost squares of the astrological fort diagram</term>. When these enter the fortress, they define the outcome through their numbers. The Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note>, Jupiter <note>here likely referring to a specific malefic aspect or a scribal variation for Mars/Saturn in this context</note>, and Rahu are proclaimed as "cruel planets" <term>Krura-graha: Malefic celestial bodies that portend conflict or destruction</term>. If these reach the inner womb of the fort, they bring various forms of ruin.

If malefic planets dominate the inner circle, there will be disease and sorrow for the people of the city. However, if "gentle planets" <term>Shubha-graha: Benefic bodies like Venus or the waxing Moon</term> occupy the inner circle, the power of the enemy's arrows is neutralized <note>original: "vinayana," lead away or subdued</note>. Through the presence of such planets, stability will be maintained. Thus, the learned astrologers analyze the specifics of the fortress's defense. These mansions are designated as being under the influence of the "Varoona" <note>Western/Watery</note> energy, which defines the stability of the inner walls.

# The Lords of Time and the Science of the Hour

## The Planetary Regents

Those who are the Lords of the Months <note>original: "māsādhipatīni"</note> have been shown here; the remaining ones were characterized by the sage Varaha <note>A reference to Varahamihira, the famous 6th-century Indian astrologer and polymath</note>. One must observe the planets as they are situated within the peaceful and agitated sectors of the wheel. In the Wheel of Time, the Moon, the Sun, Jupiter, and the Son of the Sun <term>Saturn|original: "Suryaja," literally 'born of the sun'</term> take their places. O Great Fortunate One, the courageous Mars, Venus, and Saturn <note>original: "Sairikeya"</note> follow in their proper sequence for the sake of the King's duties, as established in the various sacred scriptures <note>original: "vividhāgama"</note>.

<insert>
The cruel planet <term>malefic|astrological term for planets like Mars or Saturn</term> brings intensity to battle, creating obstacles and heat, while the auspicious planet <term>benefic|planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> brings a pale, cooling success.
</insert>

Having explained the Fortress Wheel in great detail as requested, I shall now speak of the excellent characteristics of the **Hora** <note>A unit of time approximately equal to one hour; the root of the English word "hour"</note>. In this system, if the Lord of the Hour is malefic, the hour itself is considered malefic. If a benefic planet is present but its power is seized by a malefic Lord, the result will be harsh.

## The Twenty-Four Hours

There are twenty-four hours <note>original: "chaturviṃśati," referring to the 24 hours of a full day-night cycle</note> assigned to the day and night. These are determined by the movement of the Time-Hora through the zodiac signs. To calculate the current hour, take the duration of the day, double it, and divide the measure <note>original: "nāḍyāḥ," a traditional unit of time</note> by five; the remaining portion reveals the ruling Hora.

The first Hora of any day is governed by the Lord of that Day <note>For example, the first hour of Sunday is ruled by the Sun</note>. Following that, the other six planets take their turns in a specific sequence: the **Sun**, **Venus**, **Mercury**, the **Moon**, **Saturn**, **Jupiter**, and **Mars**. 

The twenty-four hours are known by these traditional names, each granting its own specific result:

1. **Saumya** (The Gentle)
2. **Karata** (The Active/Harsh)
3. **Sthira** (The Steady)
4. **Vega** (The Swift)
5. **Arirāpalikā** (The Enemy-Protector)
6. **Rashmi** (The Radiant)
7. **Shubhā** (The Auspicious)
8. **Krūrā** (The Fierce)
9. **Satī** (The Virtuous)
10. **Kāmata** (The Desirous)
11. **Kālarātri** (The Night of Time)
12. **Radanī** (The Tusked/Sharp)
13. **Vibhramā** (The Confused)
14. **Bhramanī** (The Wandering)
15. **Shūra** (The Heroic)
16. **Sampātā** (The Colliding)
17. **Māyā** (The Illusory)
18. **Damanī** (The Subduing)
19. **Jīvadhāriṇī** (The Life-Bearer)
20. **Shoṣaṇī** (The Withering)
21. **Varṣiṇī** (The Bountiful/Raining)

<note>The manuscript list continues with variations of these qualities.</note>

These twenty-four hours provide fruits according to their names. The wise should know the sequence of the Horas starting from the day of the Sun <note>Sunday</note>, O Goddess. There are also twenty-seven further divisions related to the lunar mansions.

# The Shadow of Rahu and the Fortress Commander’s Authority

...are continuous. Therefore, listen to the truth of this, O Parvati. <gloss>Regarding the calculation:</gloss> From the beginning of the month of **Chaitra** <note>The first month of the Hindu lunar calendar, typically occurring in March-April</note>, when the Sun is joined with the lunar day, one must determine the position of **Rahu** <term>Rahu|The north node of the moon, envisioned in Vedic astrology as a shadow planet that causes eclipses and represents chaotic or sudden forces</term>. I shall now describe the fruits of that calculation. 

If a man performs his duties without this knowledge, he shall find no success. Like a shadow, Rahu surrounds everything. For those who move with the speed of Rahu, their army may face destruction; yet, when Rahu moves behind them, it grants positive results. *   **Enjoyment and Health:** A state of physical well-being.
*   **Intelligence and Contentment:** The satisfaction of the mind.
*   **Attainment of Knowledge:** Success in learning.
*   **Loss of Elephants:** A warning of military decline <note>In ancient Indian warfare, elephants were the "heavy tanks" of the army; their loss was a catastrophic omen</note>.
*   **Enemies and Desertion:** The arrival of strength or the presence of foes.

O Goddess of the Devas, I have spoken these results to you in brief. Looking at the conditions of the month, whatever work is performed will lead to certain success. This is the truth, O Great Fortunate One, revealed to you only because of my love for you. This is more secret than the most secret <note>original: "guhyād guhya taraṃ"</note>; it must be protected and hidden with great effort.

## The Inquiry into the Rahu Wheel

**The Goddess said:**
O Blessed One, Lord of Devas, you whose crest is marked by the moon! Pray, tell me of the **Rahu Wheel** <note>original: "Rahu Chakra"</note>, for I am filled with great curiosity.

Wise men must calculate the position of Rahu in this manner during times of battle. This Rahu is the movement of Time itself <note>original: "kalamathataḥ"</note>; it is the very end of Time. No one can escape it. When one is pursued by Rahu, they must stay far away, keeping it to their back to ensure their desires are fulfilled.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating the Rahu Wheel (Rahu-chakra), a complex astrological tool used by military commanders to determine the most auspicious timing and direction for battle based on the movement of Rahu, the shadow planet.</meta>

# The Ritual Construction of the Rahu Mandala

One should engage in battle only after observing the positions of the Sun, Rahu, and Mars <note>referred to as "Kuja" or the earth-born</note> in the signs of Aries and the others. When the Moon <note>original: "Nishanatha," meaning Lord of the Night</note> is favorable, one may face the enemy in combat. 

Rahu’s movement is perpetual. One must understand the Daily Rahu, the Monthly Rahu, and the Rahu of the Path. Furthermore, there is the Nakshatra-Rahu <term>Nakshatra|A lunar mansion or one of the 27 sectors of the sky the moon passes through</term>, which provides results across the three divisions of time <note>past, present, and future</note>. The Tithi-Rahu <term>Tithi|A lunar day in the Vedic calendar</term> must be abandoned during certain months and days; this is how the Rahu governing journeys is traditionally described.

### The Construction of the Grid

To calculate these influences, one must draw a mandala <term>mandala|a sacred geometric diagram or grid used for divination</term> consisting of seven vertical lines and seven horizontal lines. 

*   **In the South-East corner:** Place the syllable **Da**.
*   **In the North-West corner:** Place the syllable **Ha**.
*   **In the South-West corner:** Place the syllable **Na**, which marks the stability of the position.
*   **In the East <note>the corner of Indra</note>:** Place the syllable **Ga**.
*   **In the West:** The syllable **Ya** should be situated.

The syllables from **Ya** to **Ha** should be placed leading toward the North-East corner. These seven lines should be marked, excluding the eight vowels. From the syllable **Ta** to **Sha**, the names of the months are to be written. Then, upon the six lines, one should write the lunar days <gloss>Tithis</gloss> in their proper order.

### The Calculation of the Bright Fortnight

Using the syllables **Ka, Pa, Da, Ha, Pa, De, Ya** as the verses prescribe, one maps the bright fortnight <note>the waxing phase of the moon; original: "Sita-paksha"</note> in sequence. Starting from the syllable **Ya** in the eastern row, one should place seven markers up to the end of the lines. One should read the six markers on the triple lines accordingly. 

From the corner marked by **Ya**, the sequence must be understood. One writes the seven in order starting from **Ya**. From the first lunar day <note>Pratipad</note> onwards, combined with the syllable **Ha**, the pattern follows the ancient instructions. In this system, the syllable **Ka** corresponds to the tenth lunar day. 

<note>The following lines are partially obscured by ink smudging.</note>

If these placements occur on other lunar days, different results arise. The sounds and syllables occurring here must be carefully observed. Such are the defining characteristics of the Rahu Wheel for the guidance of the king.

Now, another method for the Rahu of the lunar days is described here, as observed in the *Ancient Manual of War* <note>original: "Purana Samara," likely referring to a lost or specific technical treatise on military astrology</note>, which provides complete insight. Through the division of lunar days, the month is expressed within the four directions. At the start of the second lunar day, and on the eighth, in both the bright and dark fortnights <term>Paksha|One of the two fortnights of the Hindu lunar month</term>, one should calculate the position of Rahu. On the eighth day, and similarly at the time of the New Moon <note>original: "Amavasya"</note>, Rahu is said to reside in the house of the Kaveri <note>A reference to the Southern region of India</note>; at that time, it is exceptionally powerful. From the first day of the bright fortnight <margin type="right">n-</margin> onwards, when the ritual is observed, if Rahu is in the West, its power is even greater. During the ninth and first periods <margin type="right">tra</margin> of the day, Rahu resides in the South, and there it is at its peak strength. If Rahu were in the East, it would be <margin type="right">hindered</margin> by its position, but in the West, it becomes auspicious. In this manner, the influences are determined by their relative positions. 3 <margin type="right">da-e</margin>

This completes the Rahu-Wheel of lunar days. On the third day, and on the eighth, the points of influence arise. On the seventh day in the meeting of the directions; on the tenth day in the sign of Sagittarius <note>original: "Dhanu-rashi"</note>; and in the North-West on the fourteenth day <note>original: "Bhuta-vasare," the day of the spirits</note>. During the bright fortnight <margin type="right">ta na</margin> on the fifth day, it rises in the West; on the eighth and eleventh days, it resides in the South-West <note>the region of Nirriti</note>. In the Southeast <note>original: "Agneyyam"</note>, for a measure of fifteen *palas* <term>Pala|A traditional unit of time, approximately 24 seconds</term>, Rahu resides without fault. In the East and other directions, it moves upward... and thus it goes toward the mountain of victory. Whether from the North <note>original: "Saumya"</note> or from the South, or where the earth is diminished... in the West <note>original: "Varunya"</note>, in the East, in the Northeast <note>original: "Isha"</note>, and in the South... in the Southeast and the Northwest <note>original: "Vayu"</note>. Through the Northeast, the South, the Northwest, the Southwest <note>original: "Nairrti"</note>, and the North—the domains of Shiva, Varuna, and the Sky—Rahu remains until midday. After midday, it does not depart through the first six directions, but returns again to the West, Agni, and Soma... as described in the regions of Indra and the Southwest.

The "Rahu of the Moments" <term>Muhurta Rahu|A specific time-bound manifestation of Rahu that affects the quality of a short period of time</term> is explained here, whose speed is measured in *nadikas* <term>Nadika|A unit of time equal to 24 minutes; two nadikas usually make one muhurta</term>. Then Abhijit, and the one called Bala (Strength). Then there is Vijaya, [name unclear], Varuna, and Bhaga. These are the fifteen moments. Some are gentle <note>original: "Saumya"</note> and some are cruel <note>original: "Krura"</note>. At midday, the moments of Bhaga and others... one who knows these moments becomes a master of time. Among them, the combinations <note>original: "yogau"</note> of Raudra and Sarabhata are associated with the Earth. Abhijit and Rahu are released; the ten-faced one <note>original: "Dashavadana," likely referring to a specific fearsome form of Rahu</note> moves into the essence. At the junctions of time <term>Sandhi|The transition points between periods of the day, such as dawn, noon, and dusk</term>, Rahu in these five moments can cause destruction. If one should happen to travel or act during these specified Rahu-moments and planetary alignments, the directions and the planets will cause total ruin.

Regarding the knowledge of these moments: it is to be understood through the shadow <term>Chhaya|The length of a shadow cast by a gnomon or a person, used to determine the time of day</term>. By measuring "steps" <note>original: "padaih," likely measuring the shadow in human foot-lengths</note> of six, five, or sixty... by the rays of the sun... in the "eye" moments <note>original: "Netra," possibly a code for the number two or a specific time period</note> at midday and in the afternoon. In the forenoon, the shadow stretches toward the West; in the afternoon, it moves toward the East. Even at midday, by the shadow of the "deer" <note>original: "mrgam," possibly a reference to a specific gnomon or a constellation-based measurement</note>, when the planet is in a single position, that is the time. [text unclear] ... the zodiac signs.

From the direction of the Fire-Consumer <note>original: "Kritabhug-disham"; the Southeast, ruled by Agni</note>, one should go to the West <note>original: "Varuni"</note> in the Southeast. From the West, one moves toward the Southwest <note>original: "Nairriti" direction</note>. In this way, during the daytime watch, the eight half-watches are calculated.

When the remainder is exhausted, one should travel in the reverse order; such is the movement of the eighth planet. Likewise, during the day, [one considers] the direction of the King of Death <note>original: "Yamaraja-disham"; the South</note>. At night, the cycle moves from the Fire-Consumer (Southeast) to the Terrible direction <note>original: "Raudri"; the Northeast</note> through the intensity of the [planetary] passion. 

The traveler's directions move from the West <note>original: "Varuna"</note>, the Fire-Consumer (Southeast), to the Southeast, and then to the North <note>original: "Kauverya"</note>, then back to the Southwest <note>original: "Nairrita"</note>, and then to the direction of Indra <note>original: "Vasavi"; the East</note>. This movement, like the gait of the Ram <note>the zodiac sign Aries</note>, is the rule for the bright fortnight <term>Shukla-paksha|The waxing phase of the moon</term>. In the dark fortnight <note>original: "Krita-pakshe," likely a variant for Krishna-pakshe</note>, one should not go in that manner, but rather according to the sequence of the Sun. One moves from the East to the direction of the Wind <note>the Northwest</note>, and from the direction of Death <note>the South</note> to the abode of Yama.

From the South, in the movement of the stars, one reaches the Terrible direction (Northeast) and the West. From the West to the Southeast, and from the Southeast to the direction of the Moon <note>the North</note>. From the East to the Southwest; such is the movement by day. At night, however, the movement is toward the North, and from the North to the direction of the Fire-Consumer (Southeast).

From the Southeast back to the East: this is the rule for a Sunday <note>original: "Suryavasare"</note>. Whether by day or night, in the bright fortnight, this is the course to be followed on earth. On this Sunday, or whichever day it may be, one must avoid certain directions depending on the rising and setting [of the planets] as they have been described. Because of this, one should travel toward the desired direction [only after calculation]. If one falls into the [wrong direction] during the bright fortnight—

<margin>The direction of the Wind <note>Northwest</note></margin>—from the East to the Southwest. In the dark fortnight, as it is known, the movement from the East to the Southwest is observed by the wise. Here, the half-watch is determined. One should not think of the Sun in that way; the half-watch is told according to the law. The half-watch is known by the rising of the Krittika <term>Krittika|The Pleiades star cluster, whose rising is used to determine the start of specific astrological periods</term>. 

One must strive to avoid <note>original: "varjaniyah prayatnena"</note> these [prohibited directions], which are terrifying in all endeavors. By following the movement of one's own auspicious stars, one achieves success in the junction of the half-watch. The calculation of time is released by the experts of the stars for the benefit of humans on earth. One who knows the Krittika avoids the [ill effects] and is led by the light of the Krittika. And in the movement...

The remaining planets govern during the night; the distinction by day has been stated. Thus, the learned person understands the governance of the Serpent <note>likely referring to the nodes Rahu or Ketu</note>... 3. In this way, the flow of time begins. Just as the Sun, Moon, Jupiter, Saturn, Mercury, and Venus govern at night, so too does this rotation occur in a sequence of five counts. 

One half-watch <term>Yamardha|A period of 90 minutes, or one-eighth of the daylight hours</term> is recognized as twelve units of time <term>Ghatika|A traditional unit of 24 minutes</term>, forming two cycles. Through this, one gains knowledge of the hours <term>Hora|An astrological hour that determines the planetary influence of a specific time</term>. Indra is associated with this, or it is said to be the Moon. 

The serpent Takshaka is the Sun <note>The text here maps divine serpents to the planets to show their influence on time</note>, Anja is the Moon, and Karkota is Mercury. The serpent known as Pannaga should be understood as Jupiter, and the Great Anja is called Venus. Shesha should be known as Mars. These are the seven serpent-planets in order. The eighth is known as Kulika <term>Kulika|An inauspicious time-spirit, often considered a son of Saturn, whose period is avoided for starting new ventures</term>, who is Rahu, the cruel planet. 

All these are proclaimed among the planets as rulers of the half-watch. Nine and sixteen are the middle units; four belong to Mars. Four and six belong to Mercury at first; these four are the lowest. For Saturn, these are six, with two in the middle and six elsewhere... 7. In two units of time <note>ghatikas</note>, twenty-one and a half are also seen. 

From the beginning of the day’s ruler until the end, following the order of six or eight, is the nature of the Saturnian hour, O Lovely-Faced One <note>original: "Varanane," a poetic address to the Goddess Parvati</note>. This should be understood and honored with effort in all undertakings. This has been told to you, O Parvati, regarding the rising of Kulika. By the rising of the Serpent, the two Rahus <note>referring to the North and South nodes of the Moon, Rahu and Ketu</note> are seen in the vision. 

Specified briefly through the contemplation of sounds and vowels, a line <note>original: "Rekha"</note> is remembered. In the inner directions, auspiciousness will occur. Where the line is curved toward the sub-directions, one should indicate full growth and prosperity in those quarters. In the west is Venus; thus, on the day of the early moon, one speaks of the northwest direction. Starting from the first lunar day <term>Pratipat|The first day of either the waxing or waning lunar fortnight</term> until the eighth day, and from the ninth day until the full moon, and thereafter... in the middle of the first day...

"Listen, O Goddess, in brief, to this which I tell you, my dear. In the palate resides 'Varuna'. These are the 'Mahamari' <note>original: "Mahamari" meaning 'The Great Pestilence' or plague</note> configurations. There is the 'Tongue of Time' <note>original: "Kala-jihva"</note>; in these ways, the Vishtis are named here. Their forms are established for the sake of achieving results. These occur on the third, seventh, and tenth lunar days in the 'Bhutabhushana' [division]; and likewise on the fourth, eighth, and eleventh <margin>on those dates</margin> in succession. One should not travel in these directions because the Vishti arises there. Should one perform a task for the sake of the Vishti while situated in that direction, there will be the heat of fever, burning, and the effects of a great fire. In 'Karala', there is fear; in 'Mahaghora', one achieves delusion, pride, or insanity. One should perform [remedies] in the direction of the Vishti during conflicts with enemies or in different countries. For the destruction of all beings, one uses the 'Tongue of Time' day. All this has been told to you regarding the **Vishti Wheel**, O Fair-faced One. The Vishtis must be avoided with great effort in all auspicious works. This concludes the Vishti Wheel, which is spoken of in all the scriptures. Through these names, one understands the directions and times..."
