The syllables beginning with 'Ka' and others are not considered eternal names, but are to be applied in sequence according to their respective groups. One should understand the specific lunar day <term>Tithi|A lunar day in the Hindu calendar, there are 30 tithis in a lunar month</term> associated with the first letter of a person's name.

If the name begins with the **Ka-varga** <term>Ka-varga|The first group of Sanskrit consonants: k, kh, g, gh, and ṅ</term>, it corresponds to the 2nd and 10th lunar days in that direction. If the name begins with the **Cha-varga** <term>Cha-varga|The second group: c, ch, j, jh, and ñ</term>, it corresponds to the 3rd and 11th lunar days. For the **Retroflex Ta-varga** <term>Ta-varga|The third group: ṭ, ṭh, ḍ, ḍh, and ṇ</term>, it is the 4th and 12th lunar days. For the **Dental Ta-varga** <term>Ta-varga|The fourth group: t, th, d, dh, and n</term>, it is the 5th and 13th lunar days. For the **Pa-varga** <term>Pa-varga|The fifth group: p, ph, b, bh, and m</term>, it is the 6th and 14th lunar days. For the semi-vowels and sibilants <note>original: "yaralava", referring to y, r, l, v, and ś</note>, it is the 7th and 15th lunar days.

These enter the different quarters of the wheel. This is the **Time-Direction Wheel of Name-Syllables**. He who is distinguished by these specific calculations and uses them properly shall conquer his enemies.

One should determine the vowel count from the name and apply it to the designated section of the chart.

Regarding the remainder: Who is the person? What is their location? Where is the thief? The path taken by the thief is known through this specific sequence. **Knowledge of the Thief** is determined by the quantity of vowels <note>original: "matra swara parimana"</note> present in the question asked. One should calculate the total; it should then be divided by seven. When the division is performed, if a specific remainder is left, it indicates the result.

If the remainder is one, two, or three, the location is easy to find. Following the sequence, one gains knowledge of the physical form <note>original: "kaya parijnanam"</note>. This divine tradition of the Seers <note>original: "deva-rishi-sampradaya"</note> is established here. By this knowledge alone, certainty is achieved. There is no trace of contradiction here, O Parvati! Therefore, by synthesizing all these methods, one understands the fruits of the question. Through the "Face of the God" <note>original: "deva-vaktra"; likely referring to Shiva as the source</note>, the mind becomes free of doubt.

The origin of the Wheel, the **Wheel of Time** <term>Kalachakra|A "Wheel of Time" system used in astrology to predict the timing of events</term>, O Parvati, as well as the **A-ka-ha-da Wheel** <note>A system of categorizing the alphabet for divination</note>, the **Throne Wheel** <note>original: "Simhasana-chakra"</note>, and the **Planet Wheel**—all these have been described. By analyzing the specific results of these wheels, one provides the fruit of the divination. All this has been stated in the <term>Swarodaya Vinirnaya|The "Decisive Determination of the Arising of Sound/Breath," a system of divination based on phonetics and respiratory patterns</term>.

Thirteen hundred names <note>or verses</note> are mentioned here. This is a supreme secret that destroys all doubts. Like a lion among animals, or like devotion among the faithful, so is this science. Through this, the **Science of the Arising of Breath** <note>Swarodaya Shastra</note> has been established in the mortal world. This Great Science of Breath, dear to Parvati and Shiva, belongs to Maheshvara <note>The Great Lord Shiva</note>. One should perform this to achieve success in one’s desired work. This was explained in detail by the Guru to Parvati; everything has been told in the <term>Swarodaya Vinirnaya</term>.

This is the supreme secret, O Goddess, which breaks all doubts. It is more hidden than the most hidden things, and must be protected with great effort. It should never be given to one of wicked character, to one who delights in cruel deeds, to an atheist <note>original: "nastikaya"</note>, or to one who lacks devotion.

<term>Jyotisha|The traditional Indian system of "science of light." It encompasses three branches: mathematical astronomy, mundane astrology (omens), and horoscopy. In a library context, it is the standard classification for any text dealing with time-keeping or divination.</term>
